# Product Owner log

One dated section per Product Owner pass, and one per inline ruling of a task run (headed `## <date>, <TASK> ruling, <phase>`, one line `- [<rule key>] <ruling> | reason: ... | probe: ...` per ruling), appended by the pass in the same commit that carries its `Product-Owner-Pass:` trailer. One line per decision: what, the backlog entry it came from, the owner approval path when `human`, `verify` or the wording, look or direction in `acceptance` changed, or "scope edit" for a scope-only acceptance edit, and why. The owner can veto any decision; the next pass reverts it and logs the veto.

## 2026-11-21 (Product Owner ruling, T3-03 item 1)

One ruling, asked by T3-03's spec check before the task is marked done (pull request 254). Entries are named by their line number in `tasks/backlog.jsonl`. No owner approval file was used: the one acceptance edit is a scope edit.

- Ruling on entry 162, part 1: T3-03 item 1 ("every native request in the app uses it") is read as Octave's own Kotlin and Java. Confirmed, no edit to T3-03. Why: the one client must never be registered with React Native's `OkHttpClientProvider` or handed to Expo, because JavaScript could then send through it, which is the header bridge `AGENTS.md` forbids (`docs/specs/T3-03.md`, "Not React Native's client"). So the clients React Native and Expo link cannot use the one client by design; for them the item can only mean a fence, and a fence is a build check, not something the client's own task can test. Decision 0005 keeps the token in Kotlin, so those clients cannot carry the header, and nothing in the app calls them today (the spec's grep of `apps/tv/src` and `apps/tv/index.js`). T3-05 item 1 routes every gateway request through the one client and T3-11 item 2 every image request. The item's text stays as it is: the task is in progress with frozen tests, and this is a reading, as with entry 157.
- Ruling on entry 162, part 2: T3-04 fences the clients React Native and Expo link into the APK. Entry 162 marked taken by T3-04, by hand, since the tool marks an entry only through an add; T3-04 carries the work that remains.
- Edited T3-04, new acceptance item 7: the check says, with a test per path, how it treats React Native's own networking (JavaScript `fetch` and `XMLHttpRequest`, `WebSocket`, a network source on React Native's `Image`, and any call to `OkHttpClientProvider`); each is refused outside the transport adapter or sits on a named allowlist with its reason. Entry 162. Scope edit. Why: item 6 (entry 150) names only Expo's paths, and item 3 refuses only a call that passes a header or token, so a header-free request through React Native's client had no fence. The paths are the files under `react-native-tvos` 0.88.1-0 `ReactAndroid/src/main/java/com/facebook/react` that take or build an OkHttp client: `modules/network/NetworkingModule.kt`, `modules/network/OkHttpClientProvider.kt`, `modules/websocket/WebSocketModule.kt` and `modules/fresco/FrescoModule.kt`. `devsupport/inspector/DevSupportHttpClient.kt` is the fifth; whether it ships in a release build is unverified, so the item does not name it and T3-04's spec should read it. Refuse or allowlist stays with the spec.
- Left for the refiner and the coordinator: `docs/specs/T3-03.md` still says T3-04's acceptance does not cover these clients, and T3-03's `evidence` still names entry 162 as a gate. Why: a pass writes no spec and sets no status field.

## 2026-11-21 (Product Owner edit: no build on a real TV before the cleartext safeguards)

One narrow edit, asked by the coordinator after the third pass. No backlog entry is taken and no task is added, moved or dropped. No owner approval file was used: every edit is to `depends_on`, which changes no acceptance, `human` or `verify`. The owner can veto any of it.

- Edited T3-17, `depends_on`: adds T3-19, T3-20 and T3-22 (keeps T3-01, T3-02 and T3-21). Entries 170 and 171 (T3-20, T3-19), 174 and 175 (T3-22). Why: the owner's standing priority of 2026-11-21 is that a self-hosted install must never become a way into someone's local network. T3-15, in flight, allows plain HTTP app-wide with the host classifier as the only gate, so from its merge every build can talk plain HTTP. T3-17 is the first task that puts such a build on a real TV on a real LAN; it now waits until the fence is checked on the built APK (T3-19) and a name that resolves outside loopback and the private LAN is refused (T3-20).
- T3-22 is in the same gate for a different reason: it is not a cleartext safeguard. Why: the first private-key install should not carry the Expo template's unused permissions (SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW, external storage) and queries onto a real device; the task is small and has no owner step.
- Edited T3-12, T3-13 and T3-14, `depends_on`: each adds T3-19, T3-20, T3-22 and T3-21. Why: each has an acceptance item that runs or measures a build on the Shield (T3-12 item 5, T3-13 item 2, T3-14 item 2), and none carried the private-key dependency the third pass gave T3-17. T3-13 and T3-14 depended only on T3-01, so either could have reached the Shield before any safeguard. They are gated on the four tasks directly, not on T3-17: the rule is that the safeguards exist, not that the owner's first check has passed.
- T3-18 is not edited. Why: it depends on T3-12 and T3-14, so the gate reaches it through them. T11-06 depends on T3-12 and inherits it too.
- Left T3-10. Why: it builds the driver that can take a device serial; its acceptance has no run on a real TV, and it runs in the CI emulator job.
- No T4 task is edited. Why: no T4 acceptance item puts a build on a real device (read: T4-01 to T4-09; T4-09's "install id" is an identifier). T4-05 keeps T3-21 and T4-02 keeps T3-22 from the third pass. The first later Shield check is in T5 and follows T3 in order.
- Cost: T3-17 now waits on T3-15 and T3-05 (through T3-19 and T3-20) and on the owner's key (T3-21), so the first Shield check comes later in T3. The gate also holds back the parts of T3-13 and T3-14 that need no device (the Baseline Profile CI check, the Perfetto trace), since a dependency gates a whole task. Nothing depends on T3-17, so no other task moves.
- Not covered: a dependency gates when a task starts, not what an agent installs by hand. T3-21 item 4 (an install onto a real TV refuses a debuggable build) is the only check in code; a refusal of any install before T3-19 and T3-20 are done would be a new rule, which is the owner's.

## 2026-11-21 (Product Owner pass, directed: T3-05 and the manifest check's attribute reading)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator: none of the four items was on `main`'s backlog, because three live on the unmerged branches `task/T3-05` and `task/T3-15` and one in a plan file. Entries are named by their line number in `tasks/backlog.jsonl`. No owner approval file was used: the one acceptance edit is a scope edit. The owner can veto any of it.

- Edited T3-05, `depends_on`: adds T3-15 (keeps T3-03, T2-04, T3-02 and T2-08). No backlog entry on `main`; the request is follow-up 1 of `docs/specs/T3-05.md` on `task/T3-05` (`ea3480e4`). Why: a release-type build refuses all plain HTTP until T3-15's network security configuration is on `main`, and every fixture server and the local test server are plain HTTP, so items 2, 3, 4, 6 and 7 cannot pass on the emulator without it; the spec's milestone 0 already stops there. ## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner pass, directed: the T3-16 and T3-05 review lows and three workflow faults)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator. Sources: `docs/plans/active/009-t3-16-review-followups.md`, `docs/plans/active/008-t3-05-review-followups.md` and the coordinator's three workflow observations. Eleven backlog lines were appended (193 to 203), since the tool adds a task only against a backlog entry; entries are named by their line number in `tasks/backlog.jsonl` on this branch. No owner approval file was used: no existing acceptance item, `human` or `verify` changes, and nothing here is on a screen. The owner's rule of 2026-11-21 applies: security lows on network, cleartext, permission and dev-tooling surfaces are fixed promptly. The owner can veto any of it.

- Added T3-24 after T3-16 (refuse the path forms the native request's path rule still passes). Takes entry 193. Depends on T3-16. Why: both T3-16 reviews found that `/Items/..;/x`, `/Items/%2e%2e;/x`, `/Items/..%00/x`, fullwidth dots, `/Items/%25c0%25ae%25c0%25ae/x` and five rounds of encoding pass `RequestPath.kt`, `request-path.ts` and C2, while `docs/security.md` says a session's call cannot leave its base path. Whether a proxy in front of Jellyfin reads any of them as a dot segment is unverified; the fix does not wait for the answer. The fix is to refuse, not to narrow the sentence: item 6 allows a narrowed sentence only for a form the spec shows cannot be refused. Item 5 guards the cost: whether any path the gateway sends holds a `;` or an escape that survives four rounds is unverified by this pass, so the accept vectors, T3-16's frozen tests and the T3-05 wire tests must pass unchanged, and a path that would be refused returns the task to the Product Owner. Model fable, as T3-16. Reading of item 1: "after any round of percent-decoding" covers the `;` as well as the control characters, so `..%3b` and `..%253b` are refused too; the examples are not the whole set.
- Edited T3-17, T3-12, T3-13 and T3-14, `depends_on`: each adds T3-24. Entry 193. Why: these are the four tasks with a run on a real TV (the real-TV gate of 2026-11-21), and T3-23 set the precedent that a security low on the network or permission surface is closed before one. ## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner pass, directed: T3-19's three readings and T3-04's frozen linked-path test)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator after T3-19's spec check: `docs/specs/T3-19.md` ("Before the Tests step") waits on three readings and one frozen-test change. No backlog entry is taken, and no task is added, moved, dropped or edited. No owner approval file was used: no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` changes, and nothing here is on a screen. The readings are logged without rewording the items, as with entries 157 and 162. This section is committed on `task/T3-19` and reaches `main` with that task's pull request; no script reads this log from `main` (`scripts/check-flow.mjs` reads it only for a task list change in the branch's own commits, and the workflow's agents read the worktree). The owner can veto any of it.

- Ruling on T3-19 item 1 ("fails when Octave's code can reach one"): read as a direct reference. The check fails when an instruction attributed to a `dev.octave.` class holds a network sink outside the one client, or references a listed entry point or hook (the spec's rules D1 and D4). It is not call-graph reachability. Confirmed, no edit. Why: `MainApplication` starts React Native, which registers `NetworkingModule` in every build, so a transitive walk is red on every build and would have to be silenced by an exception list, which is weaker than a narrow rule that can be red. The item's other half is not narrowed: every class in the APK that holds a sink must be on the named list, whoever calls it. What the reading does not see is a call from Octave's code that goes through a listed library class by a route the source rules also miss; the ESLint rules T1 to T7 and Kotlin rule K7 (T3-04) hold the source side.
- Condition on item 1: `docs/security.md` says in the same pull request that the check is direct references and not a call graph. The spec has it in "What the dex check cannot see", but the item 5 frozen test as planned pins only the native libraries, the Hermes bundle and reflection, so the review checks the sentence. If the implementer finds that D4 needs an exception list to go green, the reading returns to the Product Owner.
- Ruling on T3-19 item 2 ("removed from the release APK, or a test shows that no release code path calls it"): takes the second branch, with a gap that is stated, not closed. Confirmed, no edit. What is tested: the release dex references `DevSupportHttpClient` from no class outside a named list of dev-support callers (D6, on the real APK in `pnpm tv:apk:check`), and the release app opens no connection to the development server port while it starts (`pnpm tv:smoke`). What is not tested: that those callers never run in a release build. For `ReactHostImpl.loadNetworkResource`, entered from C++ by the inspector target, that rests on reading React Native's source (the Fusebox release flag defaults to false); whether the shipped `libreactnative.so` was compiled that way is unverified. So the item passes on a test of who can call the client and of what the app does at start-up, not on a proof that no release path calls it. Why accept it: removal needs a patched React Native (a `@DoNotStrip` method keeps the class), which is out of proportion; the client listens on nothing; and the untested caller sits behind two gates, of which the second has a test at start-up (below).
- What the item 2 gap is worth, corrected after reading the source (`react-native-tvos` 0.88.1-0, `ReactAndroid/src/main/java/com/facebook/react/devsupport/inspector/InspectorNetworkHelper.kt` and `DevSupportHttpClient.kt`): `loadNetworkResource(url, listener)` requests whatever URL the inspector passes and streams the response back to it; the URL is not fixed to the development server. So the untested caller, if it ran, would let a connected debugger make the app fetch any address the TV can reach, a home-network address included, over a client that sends no Octave header. That is the kind of path the owner's first worry names, so the gap is not minor. Two gates stand before it, both read from React Native's source: the inspector target exists only when the Fusebox release flag is on (default false; the compiled `.so` is unverified), and a debugger reaches the target only over the inspector connection, which the app itself opens to the development server address from a development or perf-test dev-support manager (a release build gets `ReleaseDevSupportManager`). The second gate is the one `pnpm tv:smoke` observes: no connection to the development server port while the app starts. It observes start-up only, and the watch on port 8081 would not see a `loadNetworkResource` request itself. The reading stands on those two gates; it does not stand on the client being harmless.
- Conditions on item 2: R5's reason in `LINKED_NETWORK_PATHS`, `docs/security.md` and the pull request's "Untested" section each say that the C++ inspector gate is read from source and not tested. The spec's backlog line 1 (read the release `.so` files for the inspector's compile flag, or another test that C++ cannot call `loadNetworkResource` in a release build) is appended by the task; the next full pass places it, and no task exists for it yet because the line is not on `main`. `release_build_has_no_inspector` stays T3-16 item 5.
- Ruling on T3-19 item 3, last clause ("`LINKED_NETWORK_PATHS` names only what the APK still holds"): read as a two-sided partition. (a) No row for a package excluded from autolinking; (b) every remaining id is either the `linkedPath` of an `APK_NETWORK_ENTRY_POINTS` row, which the dex check holds to the real APK, or on `SOURCE_ONLY_LINKED_PATHS` with a reason, never both. Confirmed, no edit. Why: `judgeLinkedClients` and T3-04's frozen tests require a row for every source file of a linked package that can open a connection, so a row whose class R8 removed cannot be dropped without blinding the source inventory. The reading keeps more of the fence, not less: the literal one would delete rows for code that is still installed and would return to the dex on the next R8 change. A source-only entry is a claim that no class of that path holds a sink in the dex; the dex check's D2 fails if the claim is false, and the `guardian` review reads each reason.
- Ruling on the frozen `scripts/linked-network-paths.test.ts` (T3-04 items 6 and 7): changed, on `task/T3-19`, with `Frozen-Test-Change: scripts/linked-network-paths.test.ts`. Why it qualifies: the file required `LINKED_NETWORK_PATHS` rows for `expo-file-system` (E2), `expo-asset` (E3), `@expo/log-box` (E4) and `@expo/dom-webview` (E5), and T3-19 item 3 has those rows removed for each package it unlinks, so the two contradict for every package that goes. T3-04 item 6 itself is still met by the changed file: it asks that each path "is refused or sits on a named allowlist", and an unlinked package's path is still refused. `docs/specs/T3-04.tests.json` is unchanged (same file list).
- What the change is. For each of the four packages, exactly one holds: it is linked and T3-04's assertions run unchanged (rows present, each with a reason and a refusing rule, each file present in the installed package and still found by the inventory); or `apps/tv/package.json` excludes it from Android autolinking and `LINKED_NETWORK_PATHS` holds no row for it, by id or by package name. Excluded packages leave the file's `REAL_PACKAGES`, which two more tests read. With no exclusion list, as on `main` today, the file asserts exactly what it did.
- What the change does not loosen. Every `expectLintRefused` and `expectKotlinRefused` line is untouched and runs in both states, so an import of an unlinked package from Octave's code still fails. E1, E6, E7, R1 to R5 and the worklets test are untouched. Only the four packages item 3 names can leave: any other name on the exclusion list changes nothing in the file (tried: `react-native` and `expo-modules-core` on the list, 17 of 17 tests still run their full assertions and pass). A package cannot be excluded on paper while its rows stay (tried: `expo-file-system` on the list with its rows present, the E2 test fails).
- Two departures from the spec's wording of the change, both tighter. First, the exclusion list is read as Expo reads it, not as a union: in `expo-modules-autolinking` 58.1.0 (`build/commands/autolinkingOptions.js`, `parsePackageJsonOptions`) `expo.autolinking.android` is spread over `expo.autolinking` and only `include` is merged, so an `exclude` under `android` replaces the top-level one. A union would call a package unlinked while Android still links it, and drop its inventory (tried: a top-level `exclude` naming `expo-file-system` beside `android.exclude: []` leaves the E2 test in its linked form). Second, the partition is limited to the four named packages, where the spec filtered `REAL_PACKAGES` by the whole list.
- Left for the refiner before the run resumes at the Tests step: `docs/specs/T3-19.md` still says union in four places (Facts, "Autolinking exclusion"; "The Product Owner change item 3 needs", first bullet; the Unlinking section's `linkedPackages` bullet; Acceptance row 3's "either key"), and calls the readings and this change "not settled". `linkedPackages` and T3-19's own frozen tests must read the list by the same override rule, or `pnpm lint:network` would stop reading a package Android still links. A pass writes no spec.
- Condition on the frozen-test change: it relies on T3-19's own frozen item 3 test requiring, for each excluded package, its Java package on the production `ABSENT_PACKAGES`, so that the dex check (D5) fails if the classes are still in the APK. If the freeze commit lacks that test, the change returns to the Product Owner. The exclusion itself is proved on the built APK, never by this file.
- For the owner (veto, not a blocker): item 2 passes with the C++ inspector gate untested, and T3-19 is one of the tasks the first build on a real TV waits for (the real-TV gate of 2026-11-21). The untested caller fetches a debugger-supplied URL (above), so this pass's recommendation is that the first real-TV install waits for the test in backlog line 1: the next full pass, once that line is on `main`, makes it a task and adds it to `depends_on` of T3-17, T3-12, T3-13 and T3-14, unless the owner says the two source-read gates are enough. It is not done here because the tool adds a task only against a backlog entry, and the entry arrives with T3-19's pull request.
- Note for the coordinator: T3-23 (PR #265) changes `scripts/tv-apk-check.mjs` and `scripts/tv-apk-manifest.mjs` and neither file of this commit; `task/T3-19` rebases on it before the Tests step, and the dex step is added after whatever `tv:apk:check` runs on `main` then.
- No reorder and no drop of an existing task.

## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: T3-19 sink rule S1)

Two linked rulings, asked by the coordinator before T3-19's implement step: spec check issue 1 (`docs/specs/T3-19.md`, "Spec check") and the test writer's open question. No backlog entry is taken and no task changes. Scope edit of the spec's rules only: no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` changes, nothing is on a screen, so no owner approval file was used. No frozen file is changed. Written into the spec as decision log entries 14 and 15 (the spec is untracked; the workflow commits it). The owner can veto either.

- Ruling (spec check issue 1): an invoke of `okhttp3.Call$Factory.newCall` whose innermost remaining frame is a `dev.octave.` class is not a sink for D1, D2 or D3. Nothing else is exempt: the same invoke in library code (also when inlined into an Octave method), any invoke on `okhttp3.WebSocket$Factory`, and every reference to `OkHttpClient` or its builder fail as before. `ONE_CLIENT_CLASSES` gains no caller. Why: `OctaveHttp.callFactory(session)` returns `okhttp3.Call.Factory` (`modules/session/android/src/main/java/dev/octave/session/http/OctaveHttp.kt:29`), T3-04 tells every caller to send through it (`scripts/check-network-paths.mjs:12`), and the frozen `sender-one-client` sample must pass (`scripts/tv-apk-dex.test.ts:130-136`); as written, the check would go red on the first correct caller (T3-05) and its message would recommend what it fails.
- Added with it, so the exemption cannot carry another factory: an Octave instruction that takes a `Call$Factory` or `WebSocket$Factory` (an invoke's return type, a field read's type) from a class outside `dev.octave.` fails D1. Why: security first. The exemption is safe only if the factory Octave invokes is the one client's; this refuses by default every other source the dex can show. No frozen sample pins it; the implementer adds an unfrozen test, and the review checks that it exists.
- Ruling (the test writer's question): yes. A descriptor or declaration that merely has the type `OkHttpClient` or `OkHttpClient$Builder` is an S1 reference: instruction operands (the referenced member's parameter, return and field types included), declared fields, a declared method's own descriptor, and superclass lines. A declaration is attributed by its member line, else by the class line. Why: refuse by default, and the first ruling depends on it. An Octave method that receives a client as a parameter and passes it on as a `Call$Factory` holds no S1 instruction, so without declarations the exemption would let it send around the session's origin, redirect and proxy policy. The cost is more library rows, each with a reason; D3 keeps them honest.
- Checked: in the frozen `release-dump.txt` the renamed types (`vr1`, `ur1`; `release-mapping.txt:241-242`) appear only in the four classes the fixture rows FX1 to FX5 cover, and in no `dev.octave.` sample (grep of the fixtures, this pass). `lh0`'s static field has no member line in the excerpt, hence the class-line fallback. Unverified: the frozen tests under these rules, since no script exists yet. Condition: if a frozen pass sample goes red under either ruling, the implementer stops and the ruling returns here; the fixture is not edited.
- What the rulings do not close, stated and not claimed: a `Call$Factory` that reaches Octave's code as a method parameter, or through a supertype other than `OkHttpClient`, from a class that names neither a client nor an invoke on the factory. Whether the APK holds such a class is unverified. Condition: `docs/security.md` names this beside the call-graph limit in the same pull request. Not pinned by item 5's frozen test, so the review checks the sentence.
- Returns to the Product Owner later: a library that Octave hands the factory to (a Media3 OkHttp data source, for the player) invokes `Call$Factory`, so it needs an entry-point row, and D4 then fails Octave's reference to it. D4 has no exception list (reading 1), and this pass adds none; the player task brings it back.
- No task added, moved, dropped or edited.

## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: T3-19 sink rule S1, two corrections)

Corrections to the section above, same day, before the implement step. The rulings stand. Scope edit; no approval file.

- Corrected wording of what the rulings do not see: a factory that reaches Octave's code as a method parameter, or typed as anything other than `Call$Factory` or `OkHttpClient` (`Object`, an erased generic) and cast in Octave's code, from a class that names neither a client nor an invoke on the factory. "Through a supertype" above was wrong. A `check-cast` to `Call$Factory` is not made a failure: a lazy holder of the one client's own factory would fail it. This is the sentence `docs/security.md` takes.
- The descriptor ruling's cost reaches D4, which the section above did not say: a class that is a row only because it declares a client-typed field or parameter is also a class Octave may not reference. Checked on the release mapping of the fixture's build (`apps/tv/android/app/build/outputs/mapping/release/mapping.txt`, same `pg_map_id` as the frozen excerpt): 22 mapping sections outside `okhttp3.`/`okio.` hold a member line naming either type; none is among the 28 non-Octave, non-JDK, non-Kotlin classes Octave's Kotlin imports. Where a class Octave calls shows up (`kotlin.jvm.internal.Intrinsics`), the lines are `OkHttpClientProvider`'s merged members and are attributed to it by member. A check by import and mapping section, not a run of the rule; unverified beyond that. Condition: if the real-APK run finds such a class, the implementer stops and it returns here; the ruling is not narrowed and D4 gets no exception.
- Spec edits of this ruling, all in `docs/specs/T3-19.md`: decision log entries 14 and 15; a pointer to them in the S1 row and in the D1 bullet; one sentence on spec check issue 1 saying where it is resolved. The rest of the spec is the refiner's and the checker's.

## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: T3-19 rule D4 and inlined library code)

Two rulings, asked by the coordinator after T3-19's implement run stopped at milestone 1 (`docs/specs/T3-19.md`, "Open questions for the Product Owner" 1 and 2; commits `3a91767c`, `56ec2b39`, `cafbc5ce`). No backlog entry is taken and no task changes. Scope edit of the spec's rules only: no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` changes, nothing is on a screen, so no owner approval file was used. No frozen file is changed and no frozen-test ruling is spent. Written into the spec as decision log entries 22 and 23; the spec is tracked on this branch since `3a91767c` and is committed with this section. This changes a security rule, so it is flagged for the owner's veto with the pull request.

- Ruling (question 1): D4 judges the reference Octave's own source makes. Each `dev.octave.` frame of a site's stack makes one reference: the instruction's operand when the frame is the innermost remaining one, else the holder of the next-inner remaining frame (the callee R8 inlined into it). D4 fails when that reference is a listed entry point or hook. This is the implementer's option (a) plus one clause; D4 still has no exception list. Why, security first: the six failing sites are Expo's `getDefaultReactHost` and `ReactHostImpl` code inlined under `MainApplication`'s lambda, whose source names only `ReactHost` and `ExpoReactHostFactory`; when R8 does not inline that code D4 never sees it, so judging it proves nothing about Octave and makes the result depend on the build. Reading 1 (direct references, not a call graph) is kept, not widened.
- Why option (a) alone was not taken: it fails open when Octave's source calls a listed class or hook and R8 inlines the callee. The invoke disappears, every instruction left has the listed class as its innermost frame, and (a) skips them; D1 fires only if the inlined body holds a sink, and a hook's setter (`OkHttpClientProvider.setOkHttpClientFactory`) holds none. The added clause reads the lost invoke's target from the inline stack, so the check refuses by default on every build.
- Why option (b) was refused: it narrows D2, which the first pass left whole; it takes `ReactHostImpl` off the list, and with it D4's refusal of an Octave reference to the class whose `loadNetworkResource` fetches a debugger-supplied URL (the path the owner's first worry names; R5); it also drops `FileSystemModule` and `DataPersistor`; and it clears this build only by shortening the list.
- Untouched: D1, entry 14's factory-source rule, D2, D3, D5, D6, the lists and every row (N8 keeps `ReactHostImpl`, X3 keeps `FileSystemModule` and `DataPersistor`).
- Checked, in a throwaway copy of the tree outside the repository with the judge's D4 loop changed and nothing committed: on the release build of the Implementation record (`pg_map_id` `c316ccec…`) the frame directly inside `dev.octave.tv.MainApplication` is `expo.modules.ExpoReactHostFactory` at all six sites (lines 1161, 1266, 1268, 1286, 1291, 1309), and `node scripts/tv-apk-dex.mjs` prints `passes (5334 classes in 1 dex files, 231 sink references in 17 listed entry points, 0 in Octave's code outside the one client)`, exit 0, with the committed production list. The three T3-19 test files give 37 passed and the same 6 failed as before (milestones 2 to 4). Unverified: a sample for the added clause (none exists; the implementer adds four unfrozen tests, listed in entry 22, and the review checks they exist), and whether R8 keeps an inline frame for every inlined call on every build.
- Conditions: `docs/security.md` says in the same pull request that code R8 inlines into an Octave method is judged by D4 as the library's (not pinned by a frozen test; the review checks the sentence). Returns here: the real-APK run still red under entry 22; R8 devirtualising an Octave interface call onto a listed class (fails closed; the implementer adds no exception); a frozen sample contradicting the entry.
- Ruling (question 2): moot, and no waiver of `AGENTS.md:121`. Milestone 1 goes green under entry 22 first; milestones 2 to 4 then run in order. If the check is still red, the run stops and returns here.
- No task added, moved, dropped or edited.

## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: T3-19 rule D4 after R8's class merging)

One ruling, asked by the coordinator at the third stop on `docs/specs/T3-19.md`, "Open questions for the Product Owner" 3 (head `afb59112`). It is the fifth ruling on T3-19's security rules and is meant to end the series, so it is flagged for the owner's veto with the pull request. No backlog entry is taken and no task changes. Scope edit of the spec's rules and of the files the task touches: no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` changes, nothing is on a screen, so no owner approval file was used. No frozen file is changed and no frozen-test ruling is spent. Written into the spec as decision log entry 27, with pointers in the D4 bullet, a D7 bullet, the files list and open question 3.

- Ruling: each claim is checked where it is exact. "Octave's source names no listed class or hook" is refused before R8, by `NetworkPathsBytecodeTest` on Octave's compiled classes. The dex check keeps D0 to D3, D5 and D6 whole and keeps D4 for the references the dex attributes exactly.
- Ruling (option (c), taken): `apps/tv/app.config.ts` gives R8 `-keep,allowobfuscation,allowshrinking class dev.octave.** { *; }`, committed with this section. R8 may rename and remove Octave's classes but not merge them with others or inline their methods elsewhere. A new rule D7 fails the dex check when a dex class holds both `dev.octave.` code and other code, and names the keep rule. Cost: dex +0.6% (+45 classes, +243 methods), measured by the native engineer. D4 passes without it; it is taken so that "Octave's code" is exact for every rule (without it 19 dex classes mix Octave's code with other code, one of them OkHttp's `CallServerInterceptor`, and code no member line covers there is judged as Octave's, D1 included).
- Ruling (D4 in the dex): a reference fails only when every original class it can name is listed. A type names every original of its dex class; a member names the holder on its own member line, or every original of the dex class when R8 synthesized the member or wrote no line; a descriptor is read by the member's own signature (entry 26) or, failing that, as types. A set that mixes listed and unlisted originals is not attributed and does not fail. D4 also reads the inline stack whole: entry 22 dropped `okhttp3.` and `okio.` frames first, which judged OkHttp code inlined into `OctaveHttp` as Octave's own.
- Condition, same pull request: the Kotlin rule gains the twelve `APK_NETWORK_ENTRY_POINTS` classes it lacks, and an unfrozen test fails when a row class or hook is not covered. Without this the D4 change fails open. The `tv` job already runs `pnpm tv:native:test` before the build and the dex check.
- Entries 25 and 26: both stay. Entry 26 keeps its exact reading and loses its "every original fails" fallback. Entry 25 keeps its D2 clause and its hook clause; its wider test for Octave's code in a merged group is no longer reached on a build that passes D7.
- Refused: options (a), (b) and (d) (each clears this build's shapes only, and (d) is entry 22's refused option again); failing closed on any merged original (it is what made the same source pass on one build and fail on the next); taking D4 out of the dex check (it contradicts acceptance item 1 and three frozen samples, and would be the owner's edit).
- Why, security first: a required check that turns red when Octave's source has not changed gets narrowed again or switched off. The refusal now sits where the answer does not depend on R8, and the dex check says in its output how many references it left to that test.
- Stable when a pull request adds Octave code: the test before R8 reads class files R8 never touches; the keep rule makes new Octave classes stand alone like the old ones; a library-only merge can no longer turn D4 red.
- Checked, in throwaway copies of the tree outside the repository with the judge changed and nothing committed, on two release builds, the reproduced build without the keep rule (`pg_map_id` `514f8085…`, from `0bf8d980` plus `bb6b2364`) and the native engineer's build of the same tree with it (`pg_map_id` `0e713087…`): with the keep rule head exits 1 on 2 D4 lines and the ruling prints `passes (5301 classes in 1 dex files, 219 sink references in 14 listed entry points, 0 in Octave's code outside the one client)`, exit 0, leaving 1 Octave reference to the test before R8; without the keep rule the ruling prints no D4 line, leaves 6, and D7 names 19 dex classes. The three frozen T3-19 test files pass; 5 unfrozen tests fail and are reversed on purpose (entry 27 lists them). None of the 169 compiled `dev/octave/` class files in the worktree names a row class or hook. Unverified: the Kotlin test with the twelve names (not run), any device run of the build with the keep rule, and that R8 honours the keep rule beyond this one build (read from https://developer.android.com/topic/performance/app-optimization/keep-rules-overview).
- Not seen, stated: a `dev.octave.` class the test before R8 does not read (a module off the app's test classpath); a merged lambda group named for an Octave class. Returns here only for that and for R8 devirtualising an Octave interface call onto a listed class; both fail closed, and the question would then be whether D4 leaves the dex check, which is the owner's.
- No task added, moved, dropped or edited.

## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner pass, directed: the T3-19 review follow-ups and the real-TV gate)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator after T3-19 merged (PR #267). Sources: `docs/plans/active/008-t3-19-review-followups.md`, the T3-19 reviews, and the backlog lines T3-19's pull request appended (204, 206 to 210). Nine backlog lines were appended (212 to 220) for the plan's lows, since the tool adds a task only against a backlog entry; entries are named by their line number in `tasks/backlog.jsonl` on this branch. No owner approval file was used: no existing acceptance item, `human` or `verify` changes, and nothing here is on a screen. The owner's rule of 2026-11-21 applies: security lows on network, cleartext, permission and dev-tooling surfaces are fixed promptly. Two of the decisions change a security rule or the real-TV gate and are flagged for the owner's veto with the pull request: T3-29 item 1 (the narrowed exemption) and the gate edit. The owner can veto any of it.

- Added T3-28 after T3-24 (test that a release build's inspector cannot fetch a debugger-supplied URL). Takes entry 204 (the C++ inspector gate) and entry 200 (the APK inspector CLI on a real or recorded APK, which waited for T3-19 to land). Depends on T3-19. Why: `InspectorNetworkHelper.loadNetworkResource` fetches whatever URL a connected debugger supplies, a home-network address included, and what keeps the C++ inspector from calling it in a release build is two source reads (`UNSTABLE_ENABLE_FUSEBOX_RELEASE`, `fuseboxEnabledRelease`; `docs/specs/T3-19.md`, reading 2). This is the recommendation of the earlier pass made a task. The acceptance names an outcome, not a method: whether the release `.so` files show the compile flag is unverified, so item 1 asks the spec to show the reading tells the two builds apart, and item 3 sends the task back here if no test can exist, so the gate below cannot deadlock silently. Model fable: it is the owner's first worry.
- Added T3-29 after T3-28 (narrow the dex check's socket exemption to client sockets and close its D0 gaps). Takes entries 212 to 218 and entry 206. Depends on T3-19. Why, item by item: (1) `ONE_CLIENT_CLASSES.S4` exempts the whole S4 kind, listening and datagram sockets included, while `DirectSocketFactory` only builds `Socket(Proxy.NO_PROXY)` and connects it (`modules/session/.../http/DirectSocketFactory.kt:12-30`); a listening socket on the TV is the owner's first worry, so the exemption shrinks to what the client needs. This narrows T3-19's rule D1, a check tightening, and is a security-rule edit open to the owner's veto. (2) D0 passes a dex with no `pg-map-id` against any mapping (`scripts/tv-apk-dex.mjs:128-136`), which fails open on a stale mapping; a truncated APK dies with a stack trace. (3) T3-04's Kotlin rule K4 names no socket, channel, provider, `MediaExtractor` or `MediaMetadataRetriever`, so the source fence has the hole the dex check just closed. (4) Entry 206: both checks find Octave's code by the `dev.octave.` prefix; the item lets the spec choose a guard over a redesign. The judge split and the stale commit citations ride along in item 6 because they touch the same files. One task, as the coordinator allowed: every item is the dex check or its Kotlin twin.
- Ruling inside T3-29, the six bare `expectFail` D0 cases in the frozen `scripts/tv-apk-dex.test.ts`: the frozen file is not changed and no `Frozen-Test-Change` trailer is spent. The cases are loose, but none contradicts its acceptance item or the spec; item 2 adds unfrozen tests that assert each message.
- Ruling inside T3-29, the stale hashes: the Product Owner does not edit `docs/specs/T3-19.md`. Item 6 has the implementer rewrite the citations against `main`; the plan's replacement hash predates the rebase merge, so none is written into the item.
- Added T3-30 after T3-29 (keep a device's address out of the TV scripts' output and count only the watch's own controls). Takes entries 219 and 220 and entry 209. Depends on T3-19, and on T3-20 (merged while this pass was in review, PR #270) and T3-26 for order. Why: `scripts/tv-dev-server-watch.mjs:81,94` and `scripts/tv-first-focus.mjs:100` pass adb's last output line into errors and into the evidence file CI uploads, and `scripts/tv-e2e.mjs:164` and `scripts/tv-cleartext.mjs:128` do the same on the console; against a real TV over network adb the serial is a home-network address. The item covers every script that runs adb, so it waits for the two tasks that were changing those files when the pass was written (T3-20: `tv-e2e.mjs`, `tv-cleartext.mjs`; T3-26: the `tv-wire` files), checked with `git diff --name-only origin/main...` on both branches. The control-phase count (a stray connection standing in for a missed control) and entry 209 (two runs on one host) are the same listener and the same counting, so they are built together.
- Added T3-31 after T3-30 (run the dex and inspector checks on the e2e APK before it is installed). Takes entry 207. Depends on T3-19, on T3-28 and T3-29 (it runs their checks), and on T3-20 for order (`tv-e2e.mjs`). Why: `pnpm tv:e2e` judges only network security on the e2e APK (`scripts/tv-e2e.mjs:153`), and T3-17 and T3-12 install that APK on the Shield.
- Added T3-32 after T3-23 (shorten the manifest check's allowed permissions and queries to what the release APK asks for). Takes entry 210 and entry 178. Depends on T3-22 and T3-23. Why: the lists are upper bounds from when `expo-file-system` was linked; the release APK now asks for 2 permissions and 0 queries (T3-19), so the surplus would let a module add a storage permission back unnoticed. It waits for T3-23, which is changing `scripts/tv-apk-manifest.mjs` (PR #265). Entry 178 is answered by T3-19's trial 3 (the manifest merger only warns on a marker that matches nothing); item 2 has the spec record whether the markers stay.
- Edited T3-17, T3-12, T3-13 and T3-14, `depends_on`: each adds T3-28, T3-29, T3-30 and T3-32; T3-17 and T3-12 also add T3-31. ## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner pass, directed: the owner's decisions of 2026-11-22, the thin slice, the workflow tasks and the real-TV gate judged again)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator in two messages. Parts A and D to G record decisions the owner made on 2026-11-22, which the coordinator relayed; they are the owner's, not this pass's, and are written down in `docs/specs/decisions-T4.md` (A) and `docs/specs/decisions-process.md` (D to G). Both files were written by this pass from the relayed words, so the owner should read them. Where each decision was placed, the task order and every gate call are this pass's and open to the owner's veto. Part B places the coordinator's velocity review, and part C rules on a T3-20 review low. Eight backlog lines were appended (226 to 233), since the tool adds a task only against a backlog entry; entries are named by their line number in `tasks/backlog.jsonl` on this branch. The tool marks an entry taken only for a new task, so `taken_by` was set by hand on the entries an edit answers (122, 161, 173, and 230 as appended), as `WORKFLOW.md` §2a allows. This pass also edits `DESIGN.md` (one copy row) and `WORKFLOW.md` (the review follow-ups rule), both on the owner's decisions.

Flagged for the owner with the pull request: T1-17 (a CI change); the ruling in part C (a security rule left as it is); the gate edges removed in part D; T3-33 gating sign-in but not real-TV installs; the `.home.arpa` reading in T4-01; and that `pnpm task next` gives the workflow tasks T1-15 to T1-20 before the slice's T3-06, so the lists in part E, not `pnpm task next`, are the slice's order.

Part A, the owner's decisions on discovery, development builds, a Unicode host and denials:

- Edited T4-01, acceptance, with owner approval `docs/specs/decisions-T4.md`. Takes entry 173. Item 2 keeps its three drops (over 4 KB, not the expected JSON, an address the host-class rule refuses) and adds the owner's rule on top: of the rest, a reply is listed only when its `Address` host is a private-LAN IP literal equal to the datagram's source address, or a `.local` name; loopback, public addresses, a literal that differs from the source and any other name are dropped. A new item 3 says a server that advertises only an https domain is not listed and can still be typed by hand. A new item has the task's PR bring `docs/security.md`'s discovery paragraph (which still gives the host-class rule alone) up to date. The other items are unchanged. Read literally from the owner's words: a `.home.arpa` name is "any other name" and is dropped; the owner named `.local` only.
- Edited T4-02, acceptance, with owner approval `docs/specs/decisions-T4.md`. Takes entries 161 and 122. A new item 8: an https address typed with a host that is not plain ASCII is refused before any request with the owner's sentence, "This address uses characters Octave can't check. Type the server's plain address instead."; the punycode (`xn--`) form is accepted; the core and the native client refuse the same addresses (today the native client refuses it as `invalid-address` and the core lets it sign in, `docs/specs/T3-03.md:195`, `docs/specs/T2-06.md` D6). Items 1 to 7 are unchanged.
- `DESIGN.md` section 10 gains the row "Sign-in, HTTPS address with a Unicode host" with the owner's sentence, in this pull request. Why here and not in T4-02's PR: section 10 is where the address entry screen's copy lives (T4-02 item 6 reads its sentences from that table), the wording is settled, and no branch in flight changes `DESIGN.md` (`task/T3-06` has no diff from `main`; T3-08 has no branch). A plain-HTTP address with a Unicode host keeps the "refused plain HTTP" row: the owner's decision is about https.
- Added T3-33 after T3-25 (let a development build sign in only to a development server). Takes entry 176, both halves. The owner's rule is items 1 and 2: loopback, `10.0.2.2` and a build-time allowlist that is never committed, with the mechanism named by the spec (the item allows an environment variable or an ignored local file). Placement and the rest are this pass's: the refusal sits in the native session module, because in a debug build the JavaScript is the part an attacker on the network can replace; item 3 keeps the release build and the wire and e2e runs as they are. Depends on T3-05, and on T3-24 and T3-25 for order (all three change `modules/session`). Model fable: the owner's first worry.
- Entry 176's second half (the debug Metro and inspector path unreachable from a build installed on a real TV) is T3-33 items 4 and 5, and is narrower than the entry reads. What already holds: T3-21 item 4 has every install script and documented command refuse a debuggable build; `pnpm tv:smoke` shows the release app opens no connection to the development server port (T3-19, `scripts/tv-dev-server-watch.mjs`); T3-28 tests the release build's inspector gate. What is left is a debug APK put on a TV by hand whose development-server host is set to a LAN address; item 4 asks for a test that such a host is refused, and returns here if no test can exist. Unverified: that React Native 0.88 lets a build fix that host; the spec reads it.
- Gate, T3-33 and real-TV installs: it does not gate them. Why: those runs install a release build, which T3-33 leaves unchanged (item 3), and T3-21 item 4, already a gate on all four real-TV tasks, is what keeps a debuggable build off the TV. Under the owner's rule of part D, point 3, the finding opens no path and leaks nothing in the build that is installed.
- Gate, T3-33 and sign-in: edited T4-03, `depends_on` gains T3-33. Why: T4-03 is the first screen on which a person can sign a build in to a server they chose (T4-02 only probes, with no credentials), so it is the first moment the owner's rule can be broken by hand, on a network where the owner's own server answers. It is on the thin slice's path (part E); it runs in the native lane while the screens are built, and the fixture servers are loopback and `10.0.2.2`, which it admits. Open to the owner's veto.
- Denials: recorded, no task changes. A denied command still stops a run; the coordinator adds the command-hygiene rule to `AGENTS.md` separately, and this pass does not edit `AGENTS.md`. T1-19 item 5 keeps it for lanes.

Part B, the workflow fixes from the velocity review (scripts and workflow only, after T1-14 as asked):

- Added T1-15 after T1-14 (resume a task at Ship and judge the green gate by check-red's own result). Takes entry 226 and entries 83, 84 and 85. Its PR changes `.claude/agents/product-owner.md`, which is the owner's to see.

They need the line `  tv:`, single-line `run:` steps in their order (`tv:build`, `tv:apk:check`, `tv:e2e:build`), `script: pnpm tv:smoke` in the first emulator step, `script: pnpm tv:e2e` in the first one after the e2e build, and the `checks` job's steps. A job-level `if:` and a retry step placed after the step it retries keep all of them; a wrapper around `script:` would not. Part C, the T3-20 review low (a spoofed mDNS answer of `127.0.0.1` for a `.local` name):

- Ruling: a loopback answer for a `.local` or `.home.arpa` name is not refused. T3-20's rule stays as it is. Open to the owner's veto, since it leaves a security rule unchanged.
- Why, security first: refusing it stops no attacker. Whoever can spoof the answer can give the address of a LAN host they hold, the TV's own LAN address included, and that answer passes the rule today; `docs/security.md` already names it as what the rule does not cover. The loopback case needs the same spoofing plus a listener on the TV, so it is the harder path to the same end. The defences that do apply are the one-time confirmation with the exact origin shown, and HTTPS.
- Why, cost: T3-20's acceptance item 1 admits loopback, its decision log settles the point ("A private-suffix name may resolve to loopback", `docs/specs/T3-20.md:279`), and its frozen JVM tests reach their servers on `127.0.0.1` through private-suffix names (`ResolvedAddressTest.kt:55` and the two files beside it). A refusal would rewrite three frozen files and a done task's acceptance for no closed path.
- Placed: the doc half. Edited T4-01, acceptance, a scope edit with no owner approval (one docs item, no rule, screen or wording): `docs/security.md` section 3 names the loopback case beside the private-host case. Takes entry 230. Why T4-01: it is the task that first puts `.local` names in front of a viewer and it already edits that section. This pass does not edit `docs/security.md`.

Part D, the owner's review follow-ups rule and the real-TV gate judged again:

- `WORKFLOW.md` now holds the rule, in §6 (the review round) and §2c ("Review follow-ups"): small findings are fixed in the same pull request; a finding that is left and gates nothing goes to one hardening task per phase; only a finding that opens a path into a self-hoster's home network or leaks local information gates an install on a real TV. It relaxes the owner's rule of 2026-11-21. `.claude/workflows/task.js` still tells the follow-ups step to log lows; T1-18 changes it.
- Added T1-18 after T1-17 (make the review follow-ups step fix small findings in the same pull request). Takes entry 231. Depends on T1-16 for order (`task.js`).
- No hardening task is made now. T3-29 and T3-32 exist, are specified one finding group each, and T3-29 is in flight; they stay as they are and lose only their gate. The next full pass puts the untaken T3 findings (entries 221 to 225 among them) in one T3 hardening task.
- T3-28 (the release build's inspector cannot fetch a debugger-supplied URL): the gate stays on T3-17, T3-12, T3-13 and T3-14. Point 3 holds: `loadNetworkResource` fetches any URL a connected debugger supplies, a home-network address included, and what keeps it from being called in a release build is untested. That is a path into the home network.
- T3-30 (keep a device's address out of the TV scripts' output): the gate stays on all four. Point 3 holds: against a real TV over network adb the serial is a LAN address, and the scripts put it in errors and in the evidence CI uploads. That is a leak of local information, and it happens at the first run.
- T3-31 (run the dex and inspector checks on the e2e APK before it is installed): the gate stays on T3-17 and T3-12, the two tasks that install the e2e APK. Point 3 holds through T3-28: the e2e APK is a second build with the same untested inspector path, and T3-31 is what runs T3-28's test on it. Edited T3-31, `depends_on`: T3-29 is removed, so T3-29 does not gate the Shield through it; T3-31 runs the dex check as it stands, and T3-29's narrowing reaches both APKs when it lands, since both call the same script.
- T3-29 (narrow the dex check's socket exemption and close its D0 gaps): the gate is removed. Edited T3-17, T3-12, T3-13 and T3-14, `depends_on`. Point 3 does not hold: no listening socket exists (`DirectSocketFactory` builds client sockets only), and the findings are that the check would miss a future one or accept a stale mapping. It tightens a check; it opens no path and leaks nothing today.
- T3-32 (shorten the manifest check's allowed permissions and queries): the gate is removed, by the same four edits. Point 3 does not hold: the release APK asks for 2 permissions and 0 queries; the longer lists are an upper bound that would let a later change through, not a path or a leak.
- Not judged again: T3-21 (the owner's signing key, not a review finding), and T3-23, which is in review as PR #265 and is also a dependency of T4-02. By point 3 it would not gate (a check that can be fooled, no path); it is left because it is about to merge. T3-19, T3-20, T3-22 and T3-24 are done.
- New edges from this pass: none onto a real-TV task. T3-33 is judged in part A.

Part E, the thin slice comes next (owner): sign in by a typed address, browse Home's rows, play one file directly, on the emulator against the fixture servers.

Edited T8-03, `depends_on` gains T5-03, so that the order holds once a lane runner reads dependencies: item 7 cannot pass before Home exists. T8-04 (the player screen's controls) is the first task after it.

Items 1 to 8 are unchanged; item 1 is tested with a fake source. A third edit corrected the section number in item 9.
- Edited T4-01, `depends_on` and acceptance, a scope edit: it now depends on T4-02, and a new last item wires the native method into that screen's discovery source, since the screen exists first. Moved T4-01 after T4-04.
- Moved T3-06, T3-08, T3-09 and T3-11 to follow T3-24, ahead of T3-28. Why: `pnpm task next` walks the file, and the slice's T3 tasks sat behind every hardening task. T3-07 (screen captures) and T3-10 (the scripted focus test) stay where they were; they are not on the slice.

It walks the file and returns the first ready task, and the six workflow tasks sit after T1-14 as asked: T1-15, T1-17 and T1-20 are ready now, and T1-16, T1-18 and T1-19 follow as each one before it passes. Only then comes T3-06. Phases also keep their order in the file, so the hardening tasks of T3 come before T4. Until T1-19 gives tasks a lane, the lists above are the slice's order and the coordinator starts its tasks by id; the workflow tasks run as a lane of their own beside it.

## 2026-11-22, T1-16 ruling, Implement

Frozen-test ruling on four tests of `.claude/workflows/task-rulings.test.mjs` that the implementer reported as wrong. Scope edit of test fixtures only: no assertion changed, and no acceptance item, spec row or task-list entry is edited. All four are changed, because each contradicts the spec's own contract; `node --test .claude/workflows/task-rulings.test.mjs` gave 45 of 49 passing before and 49 of 49 after.

- [frozen test: item 1, technical implement question merges] Changed: added `'implement (after ruling)': work()` to the answers | reason: the spec (row 2) fixes the rerun label as `${label} (after ruling)`, and this test itself requires that key in the call list; the harness answers by label and returns `null` for a key not in its table, so the run could never end `merged` as the same test and row 1(b) require. The table, not the workflow, was wrong | probe: ran the file; the test failed with outcome `failed` before the edit and passes after.
- [frozen test: item 1, frozen problems plus a question] Changed: added `'implement (after ruling)': work()` | reason: the same cause; row 1(f) wants one ruling and then the run going on, and the rerun had no answer | probe: as above.
- [frozen test: item 2, second round and follow-ups] Changed: the third `owner ruling` answer now names `rule-c` (the answers are `rule-a`, `rule-b`, `rule-c` by call) | reason: the follow-ups step asks about `rule-c`, and the fixture answered `rule-b`. The spec (Spec check resolution 3, and the frozen item 4 test) ends a task `blocked` when an asked key has no matching answer, so the fixture contradicted the spec and made `follow-ups (after ruling)` unreachable | probe: as above.
- [frozen test: item 4, matched answer by ruleKey] Changed: added `'implement (after ruling)': work()` to the matched run | reason: the same cause as the first; the matched half requires `merged` and reads the `implement (after ruling)` prompt, but the table gave that call no answer | probe: as above.
## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: T8-22's frozen fixtures, item 6, rule L5 and the subtitle message)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator after T8-22's spec check (`docs/specs/T8-22.md`, "Spec check", issues 1 to 5 and 7) and before its Tests step. No backlog entry is taken and no task is added, moved or dropped. One scope edit of T8-22's acceptance, without an owner approval file: it changes what the task covers, not copy, look or direction, and `human` and `verify` are unchanged. Three frozen T2-05 files are changed in this commit. Written into the spec as "Product Owner decisions" 1 to 6, with a pointer at each line they change; the spec is untracked and the workflow commits it. This section is committed on `task/T8-22` and reaches `main` with that task's pull request. The owner can veto any of it.

- Ruling on the frozen T2-05 fixtures: changed, in this commit, with one `Frozen-Test-Change` trailer per file. Four fixture lines, no assertion: `packages/core/src/player/coordinator.test.ts:18` (`https://media.test/video` to `https://media.test/Videos/one/stream?Static=true`) and `:103` (`https://media.test/video?other=1` to `https://media.test/Videos/one/stream?Static=true&PlaySessionId=session-2`), `coordinator-retry-position.test.ts:16` (to `https://media.test/Videos/one/stream?Static=true`), and `coordinator-audio-pick.test.ts:17` (the static URL for a static plan, `https://media.test/videos/one/master.m3u8` for an HLS one). Why it qualifies: the fixtures contradict T8-22 item 3. Each launches a URL that is neither of the two shapes, so the tests would require the coordinator to launch what item 3 has it refuse, and no shape rule passes `https://media.test/video`. What the tests assert is unchanged; `coordinator.test.ts:130-131` compares against `plan.streamUrl`, not a literal. Checked: the three files pass on this branch with the edits (56 tests), before any T8-22 code. This is T8-22's one frozen-test ruling. The test writer does not edit the three files again; it lists them in `docs/specs/T8-22.tests.json` so `pnpm lint:flow` holds them to this version.
- Edited T8-22, `acceptance` item 2 (scope edit; entry 117): "a MediaBrowser or Token= substring anywhere in it" becomes "a Token= substring anywhere in it, or a MediaBrowser substring in its query". Why: the host and the base path of every launch URL come from the address the user typed at sign-in, with the query and fragment removed (`packages/core/src/jellyfin/gateway.ts:115-116`, `:199`), so they cannot carry a token, and `MediaBrowser` alone is a marker, not a secret. Matching it there protects nothing and would refuse every play, with one unexplained sentence, on a server whose name or base path holds `mediabrowser` (Jellyfin's former name; that Jellyfin still serves a `/mediabrowser` prefix is unverified). A header pasted into a path is still refused by its `Token=`. This narrows a rule that does not exist yet; no existing check is loosened. It is a security-rule choice, so it is flagged for the owner's veto.
- Ruling on T8-22 item 6 ("refuses to launch a negotiated plan"): read as a refusal by `launchRequest` of a converted plan. Confirmed, no edit. (a) A refused direct play sends nothing: the frozen `coordinator-launch.test.ts:70-93` forbids any report, no start was reported, and no other path sends anything for a static play session; that a server holds nothing for it is unverified. (b) When `gateway.sessionId()` throws, the coordinator sends no stop. Why: `sessionId()` throws only from `requireSession()` (`gateway.ts:353-355`, `:1064-1067`), and `stopActiveEncoding` begins with the same call (`:963-965`), so the stop can never be delivered; asking for it only raises "Stopping the server conversion failed." beside the sign-in error, a second on-screen message in a new circumstance. Entry 119's "sessionId throws" case is met by this reading; a test pins that no stop and no reporting warning follow.
- Ruling on the subtitle message (spec check issue 7): the parse failure stays first. `buildSubtitleUrl` parses, then applies the two host rules to the server's string as written, so an unparsable `DeliveryUrl` keeps "Jellyfin returned an invalid external subtitle URL." and no input changes its sentence.
- Adopted from the spec check, issue 3(a): item 3 gains a case that passes every accepted recorded plan through `launchRequest` and expects no refusal, so the rules are tied to what the gateway builds.
- Confirmed, no edit: the spec's D1 (`serverUrl` forwarded as the href without trailing slashes), as the spec check found.

## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: two frozen tests the T8-22 implementer reported)

A directed ruling, asked by the workflow after T8-22's implementation (`frozenTestProblems`, evidence on the task: commit `1d925488`). No backlog entry is taken, no task is added, moved or dropped, and no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited; both are scope edits of test data, with no owner approval file. `WORKFLOW.md:80` says the Product Owner rules once per task and the spec calls `0e2e82c5` the task's one ruling; this is a second, because the first ruling's list of frozen fixtures missed one line and the second file did not exist then. Two frozen files and the manifest change in this commit, one `Frozen-Test-Change` trailer per path. The owner can veto any of it.

- Ruling on `packages/core/src/player/coordinator-play-method.test.ts:134` (frozen by T2-05): changed, fixture data only. The case "never asks for a direct-play session" built its static plan from the HLS one and kept `streamUrl: "https://media.test/videos/one/master.m3u8"`; the override now also sets `streamUrl: "https://media.test/Videos/one/stream?Static=true"`. Why it qualifies: it contradicts T8-22 item 3 the same way the four fixture lines of the first ruling did. A static plan with a `master.m3u8` URL is neither of the two shapes, so the test would require the coordinator to launch what item 3 has it refuse (rule S1, `docs/specs/T8-22.md:121`). The assertion (no `sessionPlayMethod` call) is unchanged. The first ruling and the spec check (`docs/specs/T8-22.md:267`) listed `coordinator-play-method.test.ts:22` as passing S2 and missed the static override at `:134`. Checked: with T8-22's code the case failed with `LaunchRefusedError`, and it was the only failure of `pnpm --filter @octave/core test` (1 of 3053); with the edit the file passes (8 tests).
- Added `packages/core/src/player/coordinator-play-method.test.ts` to `files` in `docs/specs/T8-22.tests.json`, so `pnpm lint:flow` holds it to this version, as it does the three files of the first ruling.
- Ruling on `packages/core/src/player/coordinator-launch-refusal.test.ts:53` (frozen by T8-22): changed, a type only. The `stopActiveEncoding` mock is now `vi.fn(async (_playSessionId: string, _signal?: AbortSignal) => undefined)`, the port's signature (`packages/core/src/player/playstate-reporter.ts:19`). Why it qualifies: the mock took no parameters, so `call[0]` at `:86` failed the root `tsc -p tsconfig.json` with TS2493, and the task's `verify` (`pnpm check`, which runs `pnpm typecheck`) could not pass with any implementation, since the mock is in the frozen file. The test contradicts the spec in that one respect: the spec has the stop called with the play session id and a signal. No assertion and no behaviour changes; the four cases pass before and after. This is a judgment call on "contradicts", so it is flagged for the owner's veto.
- Checked after the edits: the two files pass (12 tests), the root `tsc -p tsconfig.json` prints nothing, and `pnpm lint` is clean.
- Not ruled, left with the implementer: `tsc -p packages/core/tsconfig.json` fails with TS2339, "Property 'href' does not exist on type 'URL'", five times in `packages/core/src/player/launch-request.ts`. That is implementation code, so `pnpm check` stays red until it is fixed there or in `packages/core/host-globals.d.ts`.
- The PR's Frozen tests section must name this change and the four lines of the first ruling by hand where `check-flow.mjs --json` does not list them.
## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner ruling, directed: T9-07's narrowed readings)

One ruling, asked by the coordinator after T9-07's spec parked on its open question 1 (`docs/specs/T9-07.md`). No backlog entry is taken and no task is added, moved or dropped. One scope edit of T9-07's acceptance, without an owner approval file: it changes what the task covers, not copy, look or direction, and `human` and `verify` are unchanged. No frozen file is changed. Written into the spec as "Product Owner decisions" 1 to 4, in place of the open question; the spec is untracked and the workflow commits it. This section is committed on `task/T9-07` and reaches `main` with that task's pull request. The owner can veto any of it.

- Edited T9-07, `acceptance` items 1 and 3 (scope edit; entries 67 and 69). Item 1 now names what "a bare host" is (a dotted name, an IPv4 or an IPv6 address) and sends a single label with no port to the backlog. Item 3 now hides a spaced path up to its last word with a separator or a file extension and sends the last word of a spaced path with no extension to the backlog. Why: no lexical rule tells `nas` in `connect to nas failed` from a word, or `Movies` in `cannot open /home/u/My Movies now` from the sentence after the path. This is option A of the spec.
- Not taken, option B (hide from a path's start to the end of the line). Why: it does nothing for the single-label host; it removes the cause after every path (`No such file`), which is what the reason is read for; it changes four rows of `packages/core/src/safe-reason.test.ts:12-15`. What it would hide, one word of a folder name, is not on the owner's list of local information (`WORKFLOW.md`, "Review follow-ups", 2026-11-22).
- Not chosen here, option C (show no native reason text, only fixed copy per error code): it changes what the player shows, so it is the owner's. This pass recommends it to the owner. Why: it is the only option that closes the hostname leftover, a hostname is on the owner's list, `AGENTS.md` already says "Map errors to plain copy", and every lexical filter of third-party text will keep leaving a residue (the spec's Risks name four after this task).
- The leftovers: the implementer appends four backlog lines (the spec's Risks), and no test may assert that a leftover stays visible. The single-label line names the one lexical lead, a resolver's fixed frame such as `Unable to resolve host "nas"` (unverified that such text reaches `safeReason`).
- Real-TV gate: none added, and no `depends_on` changes. Which of the two conditions of the owner's rule would hold: a leak of local information (a hostname), not a path into the home network. Why it does not gate today: no screen reads the failure message on this branch (`failureAction` in `packages/core/src/player/session-failure.ts:9` is the only caller of `errorReason`; a grep of `apps/tv/src` finds no reader), so the text goes to session state only. It would count from the first screen that shows it, since a screenshot in a bug report carries it. For the owner: decide option C, or have a full pass make the single-label backlog line a dependency of that screen's task, before that screen is installed on a real TV. Which task that is was not looked up in this pass.
## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner pass, directed: the owner's answers from the decision inbox)

A directed pass, asked by the coordinator, that records eight answers the owner gave in the decision inbox on 2026-11-22 and places what follows from them. The owner's words are in `docs/specs/decisions-owner-inbox-2026-11-22.md`; every acceptance edit below that rests on an answer names that file as its approval. Everything else here (placement, dependencies, the new tasks' acceptance, what was derived or not verified) is the Product Owner's, and the owner can veto any of it. Three tasks are added: T1-21, T8-25 and T9-08. Four more are drafted below and not added, for the reason of the next heading. No task is dropped or moved. `DESIGN.md` and `PRODUCT.md` are edited in this commit, because the acceptance of T5-01 and T9-01 asks for the answers there. T5-01 and T9-01 are marked done in a commit of their own, through `pnpm task set`.

Files of tasks in flight are not touched (apps/tv UI, the core's Home rows, `scripts/tv-apk-*`, `modules/session`, `.claude/workflows`). T5-02's acceptance is not edited.

### The size of tasks/prd.json: four tasks deferred

- Found: `pnpm check` fails when `tasks/prd.json` is over 250,000 bytes (`scripts/task.mjs:9`, tested in `scripts/task.test.ts:237`). The file was 244,223 bytes on `main`. This pass's first draft, with seven new tasks, made it 255,945 bytes and `pnpm check` failed with "tasks/prd.json is 255945 bytes, over 250000; trim or move finished phases in a Product Owner pass".
- That message asks for something this pass cannot do: `scripts/task-owner.mjs` refuses to drop a done task and has no operation that moves a phase out, and the rules at the head of the file say only "There is no done.json". Raising the limit is not the Product Owner's: it is an acceptance item of T1-13 and a frozen test pins it.
- Decision: stop at about 248,000 bytes, so about 2,000 are left on `main` for the evidence that tasks in flight write when they finish (up to 600 characters each). The file is 247,542 bytes after this pass's operations, before the evidence of T5-01 and T9-01. Filling the file to the limit would have turned the next `pnpm task set` on any branch red.
- Added T1-21 before T1-19 (let a Product Owner pass move finished phases out of `tasks/prd.json`). Backlog entry: "Give the Product Owner a way to move finished phases out of tasks/prd.json." (appended by this pass). A scope decision; no approval file. It changes `scripts/task-owner.mjs`, as T1-19 will, so the two run in series; no dependency expresses that and the coordinator orders them. It is first in line of the two because every later pass and every finishing task needs the room.
- Deferred, as untaken backlog lines with their drafts under "Drafted, not added" below: the Continue watching window and cap, the Tabler icons, the Signal loader, and the display preferences. The next pass adds them as written once T1-21 has made room, or the owner says to raise the limit.
- For the owner or the coordinator: the limit is about four finished tasks away. T1-21 is the fix this pass could place; raising the limit is the other one.

### 1. The application ID

- Edited T3-21, `acceptance`, with owner approval (`docs/specs/decisions-owner-inbox-2026-11-22.md`, 1): a new last item changes the application ID to `cloud.jellyscale.octave` in the one place that defines it, with every test, fixture and document that holds the old ID; the Shield uninstall step now also names a build with the old ID. `human` and `verify` are unchanged, and `verify` already builds and checks the APK. Why T3-21 and not a task of its own: the brief allows either, T3-17 already depends on T3-21, the two changes meet at the same first install, and a task of its own costs about 1,000 bytes the file does not have. After an install, a changed ID leaves the old package on the TV as a second app, so the change goes ahead of the Shield check.
- Edited T11-01, `title`, `depends_on` and `acceptance`, with owner approval (the same file, 1). The item "Owner decision: the final Android application ID" is answered and becomes a statement of the ID; the item "The ID is changed in the one place that defines it" moved to T3-21. The store listing and the publishing account stay. It now depends on T3-21.
- Checked: the ID is defined once, `ANDROID_APPLICATION_ID` in `packages/core/src/domain/app-identity.ts:6`. It is not written in one place, though: `git grep -l "dev\.octave\.tv"` lists 32 files outside the specs and the task files, most of them tests and fixtures under `scripts/` (many are `scripts/tv-apk-*.test.ts`), plus `docs/security.md`. T3-21's new item covers them. T3-21 therefore runs in series with T3-29, T3-31 and T3-32, which change the same scripts; the coordinator starts it when none of them is in flight.
- Placed by this pass, not the owner: the Kotlin package names under `dev.octave` stay. An application ID and a package name are separate in an Android build (unverified against this build's Gradle files; the spec shows it).

### 2. The icon set

- T3-08 and T3-09 are in flight and keep the icon slot until the set lands.

### Drafted, not added

Each is an untaken backlog line of 2026-11-22. The next pass adds it as written here, with the next free id of its phase, once the file has room.

- **Age titles out of Continue watching and show at most 20.** Phase T5, after T5-02; Catalog, sonnet, `jellyfin-api`, not `human`; depends on T5-02; verify `pnpm --filter @octave/core test`. T5-03 and T9-03 then depend on it. Acceptance: (1) Continue watching leaves out a title whose `UserData.LastPlayedDate` is older than the window, computed on the device with the Clock port; the row's requests ask for that field, and nothing on the server is hidden or changed. (2) The window is an input of the row: 30, 90, 180 or 365 days, or Never; 90 days when the account has none set. (3) The spec says which date a next-episode card ages by and what a card with no date does, each with a test. (4) An aged-out title is only hidden, and a title played again is back in the row on the next load. (5) The row shows at most 20 cards, most recently played first, after ageing and merging; this replaces T5-02's 16. (6) Contract tests cover Jellyfin 10.11, 12.4 and Jellyscale fixtures.
- **Adopt the Tabler icon set behind the IconComponent type.** Phase T3, after T3-06; App Shell & Presentation, sonnet, `change-review`, `human`; depends on T3-06; verify `pnpm check`. Takes line 237 and the Tabler line. Acceptance: (1) Icons come from Tabler's font (`@tabler/icons-webfont`, MIT) through expo-font; no native module and no other icon package is added. (2) The spec records the maintenance check, the pinned version, the licence, and which font files ship and what they add to the APK. (3) One typed map gives each icon name its glyph behind `IconComponent`; no screen names a codepoint, and a test fails for a name with no glyph. (4) The control tests' test glyphs are replaced, the played mark's check among them. (5) Owner step: the stroke weight, chosen from 1920×1080 captures. (6) The font's MIT notice is in what T11-05 gathers.
- **Build the Signal loader and the bars mark.** Phase T3, after the icon task; App Shell & Presentation, sonnet, `change-review`, `human`; depends on T3-06; verify `pnpm check`. Acceptance: (1) Owner step, before the Tests step: the owner confirms the exact motion (the pulse's period, the rings' number and travel, when each letter lights, what happens once octave is fully lit) and the brass colour from the coordinator's logo page, round 7; both become design tokens. (2) The loader is the D1 bars: the brass bar pulses and sends out rings, and each pulse lights one more letter of octave, o first (`DESIGN.md` 7.17). (3) A bars-only small form is used where space is tight; the spec lists which place uses which. (4) It is one shared component and replaces the ring wherever `DESIGN.md` shows a loading indicator. (5) Reduced motion shows the static mark with "Loading…", and nothing flashes more than three times a second. (6) The status sentence beside it is unchanged. (7) The PR holds 1920×1080 captures of each form.
- **Keep the account's settings on the server's display preferences.** Phase T9, after T9-02; Server Connection & Identity, sonnet, `jellyfin-api`, not `human`; depends on T9-02 and T2-08; verify `pnpm check`. T9-03 then depends on it. Acceptance: (1) The per-account settings (subtitles, auto-play and its countdown, the skip modes, the Continue watching window) are read from and saved to the account's display preferences on the server, through the gateway only. (2) The spec shows from recorded answers how the record is addressed and that Jellyfin 10.11, 12.4 and the pinned Jellyscale keep what is written; if one does not, the task stops with the question and files nothing itself. (3) Values read pass T9-02's allowlist; a save changes only Octave's own keys and writes no token, URL or play-session id. (4) A failed read uses the defaults, and a failed save is reported to the caller so the screen can put the old value back. (5) Switching accounts switches the settings, and a second device on the same account reads what the first saved. (6) Contract tests cover the three servers.
