## 2026-11-22 (Product Owner pass, directed: the owner raised the size limit of tasks/prd.json to 350 KB)

A directed change, asked by the coordinator, that carries out one answer the owner gave on 2026-11-22: "Raise to 350 KB now". The owner's decision is in `docs/specs/decisions-prd-size-limit.md`, and both acceptance edits below name that file as their approval. No backlog entry is taken and no task is added, moved or dropped; `human` and `verify` are unchanged. The owner can veto any of it.

- Why now: `tasks/prd.json` is 248,185 bytes on `main` (`wc -c`), 1,815 under the old limit of 250,000, and each finished task's evidence adds up to 600 characters. About three more finished tasks would have made `pnpm check` fail on every branch. The pass of earlier today named the two fixes (T1-21, or a higher limit) and said that raising the limit is not the Product Owner's; the owner has now chosen it.
- Edited T1-13, `acceptance` item 5, with owner approval (`docs/specs/decisions-prd-size-limit.md`): "over 250 KB" becomes "over 350 KB". Backlog entry: none. The other six items are unchanged. T1-13 is done and stays done; its status fields are not touched.
- Edited T1-21, `acceptance` item 3, with owner approval (the same file): "The 250,000-byte limit is unchanged" becomes "The 350,000-byte limit is unchanged". Backlog entry: none (T1-21 took "Give the Product Owner a way to move finished phases out of tasks/prd.json." earlier today). Why: the item's purpose is that T1-21 makes room by moving phases out and not by changing the limit; it now names the limit that is in force. T1-21 still runs next and brings the file back down.
- Changed with it, in the same commit: `MAX_FILE_BYTES = 350_000` in `scripts/task.mjs:9`; the boundary test in `scripts/task.test.ts:237` (350,000 bytes pass, 350,001 fail); and `docs/specs/T1-13.md` (the goal, the facts and row 5), which now gives the new limit and the date of the owner's change.
- On the frozen-test question: `scripts/task.test.ts` is listed in no manifest. There is no `docs/specs/T1-13.tests.json`, and a grep of `docs/specs/*.tests.json` for `task.test.ts` finds nothing (T1-13 predates the frozen-test flow). So the commit carries no `Frozen-Test-Change` trailer. The earlier section of today says "a frozen test pins it"; that was loose wording: a test pins the value, but no manifest freezes the file.
- Not changed: the size message ("trim or move finished phases in a Product Owner pass"), the 600-character evidence limit, the `rules` of `tasks/prd.json`, and the backlog line T1-21 took, which still quotes 250,000 bytes as the limit it was written under.
- The four tasks drafted and not added earlier today ("Drafted, not added") are not added here: this change was directed to the limit only. The next full pass can add them, since the file now has room.
- Checked: the file is 248,185 bytes after the edits (the two edits change no length).

- T1-21 -> `workflow`. Backlog entry: none (placement edit). Why: it changes `scripts/task-owner.mjs` and the task CLI, which the lane table of `docs/specs/T1-19.md` puts in `workflow`, as every other T1 Process task is.
- T9-08 -> `ui`. Backlog entry: none (placement edit). Why: human tasks do not share one lane; each sits where its work lands. - T8-25 -> `core`. Backlog entry: none (placement edit). The coordinator asked for a choice between `ui` and `player`; this ruling takes neither, by the coordinator's own first rule (the files the task will touch), and the coordinator or the owner can move it with one `edit {lane}`.
  - What it touches, as read on this branch: the failure a screen reads is built in `packages/core/src/player/session-failure.ts:9` (`failureAction`, the only caller of `errorReason` outside `packages/core/src/safe-reason.ts`), and `git grep` finds no reader of it under `apps/tv/src`. `modules/` holds only `session`: there is no player module and no Kotlin for this task to change. The rest is `DESIGN.md`'s copy table and `docs/security.md`.

The work is TypeScript in `packages/core`, which is the `core` row of the lane table ("redaction rules" among it), and its nearest relatives T9-07 (the text filters) and T8-22 (hardening under `packages/core/src/player`) are in `core`.

If the spec finds that the task must change native code, the runner's switch makes it wait for the serial slot and logs the mismatch, and the task should then be moved to `player`.

## 2026-11-22, T4-09 ruling, Check

An inline ruling on one scope question from T4-09's spec check (`docs/specs/T4-09.md`, "Spec check", issue 1). No task is added, moved or dropped, no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited, no frozen test changes and no backlog line is appended. It changes what a `pnpm tv:e2e` pass on a real TV covers, so it is flagged for the owner's veto.

- [t3-17 acceptance 2] Option (b): on a device that is not positively an emulator, the install-id stage is skipped and `pnpm tv:e2e` still passes on its other stages. Conditions, all part of the ruling: (1) the stage runs only when the device is positively an emulator; a real device and a device that cannot be told apart are both skipped, and the skip path runs no `pm clear` and no `bmgr` command; (2) the skip is written to `release-evidence/tv/e2e-install-id.txt` as one fixed line that says the stage was skipped because the device is not an emulator, with no serial and no mark, and is printed in the command's output; (3) a skip never counts as proof of T4-09: in the CI `tv` job a skipped stage fails the command, and T4-09's evidence must show the ran-stage result line (`relaunch=same clear=new restore=new transport=selected backup=...`), not the skip line. The refiner picks the mechanism without editing `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the marker the first CI run sets for its retry check is not usable, because the second run does not set it; (4) the guard and the skip-or-fail decision are a pure function with tests, written once milestone 1 has the property's words (spec check issue 2). This supersedes the spec's "The refusal is an error; `pnpm tv:e2e` fails" (The device stage, step 1), the `docs/commands.md` wording "so it runs only on your own emulator" (the command runs on a real TV, without that stage), and the spec's second backlog line ("decide how `pnpm tv:e2e` should run on a real TV"), which is now answered and is not appended. The refiner edits the spec; T3-17 and T4-09 stay as written in the task list | reason: (a) needs T3-17's acceptance or `verify` edited and (c) needs T4-09's `verify` and the `tv` job edited; a `verify` edit needs an owner approval file and none names either task, so only (b) can be ruled inline. (b) also fits what both tasks ask: T3-17 item 2 gives its own purpose, the conformance suites under Hermes on Android 11, which the install-id stage is no part of, and T4-09 item 2 asks for "an emulator test". Skipping is also the safer path on the owner's TV: nothing clears the app's data or changes its backup settings there. The cost of skipping in place of failing is a silent pass when an emulator is not recognised, which condition 3 closes | probe: ran `node scripts/task.mjs show T3-17` and `show T4-09`: T3-17 is `todo`, `human: true`, item 2 "Shield check (owner): pnpm tv:e2e passes on the Shield, so the core's conformance suites run under Hermes on Android 11", `verify` "pnpm tv:smoke && pnpm tv:e2e", and T4-09 is not in its `depends_on`; T4-09 item 2 ends "in an emulator test" and its `verify` is "pnpm check && pnpm tv:native:test && pnpm tv:e2e". Read `docs/specs/T4-09.md:176`: "The refusal is an error; `pnpm tv:e2e` fails with \"the install-id stage runs only on an emulator\" after its other stages have passed and written their evidence", and `:40`: the emulator property is "[unverified]" and the guard "refuses when it cannot tell". Read `docs/specs/decisions-process.md` ("Review follow-ups (T3-17, ...)"): it sets which findings gate an install on a real TV and says nothing on how `pnpm tv:e2e` runs there; `grep T3-17 docs/specs/decisions-*.md` finds only that heading. Read this log's fifth-pass line "no T4 acceptance item puts a build on a real device". Read `.github/workflows/ci.yml:250-281`: both `tv` runs call `pnpm tv:e2e` on an `android-tv` API 36 emulator; only the first sets the started-marker variable.

## 2026-11-22, T3-08 ruling, Check

One inline ruling on the second Check step's question for T3-08 (`docs/specs/T3-08.md`, second spec check, finding 1). It adds a control run to the run order of "Product Owner decisions" 2 and corrects two facts of that ruling. No backlog entry is taken, no task is added, moved or dropped, no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited, and no frozen file is changed. The spec is not edited here. The owner can veto any of it.

- [docs/specs/t3-08.md:product owner decisions (2, order of runs)] Yes: the control runs first, after the script check (0) and before the baseline (1). No Shield run by an agent comes before the owner is asked. (a) What runs: the spike's optimisation-pass APK, arm `F4-dd150` (the flags of the Shield run's `device.json`) and arm F1 (the flags of `results/opt-focus-F1-*/device.json`), on the implementer's own emulator, with the same `monkey -f` script as milestone 5: 12 Right bursts each of 49, 20 and 15 presses on row 0, each from card 0. (b) Which APK: `dist/browse-optpass-v5.apk` in the spike branch's worktree, installed only if its sha256 starts `54539d87442dd634`. It is not rebuilt: if the file is missing or the hash differs, the control is recorded as not run, the baseline goes ahead, and any stop-and-ask says so. (c) What makes a run count: its `BOOT arm=` line names the arm and, for F4, `"rowDrawDistance":150` (the app falls back to stock without a word when it cannot fetch its arm); the log shows each burst starting on `0,0`; the achieved gap between presses is recorded beside milestone 5's. ## 2026-11-23, T8-25 ruling, Check

Focus: one inline ruling inside a product task (T8-25, a screen shows only Octave's fixed sentence for each error code, the owner's rule of 2026-11-22); it adds no hardening or process work and moves no lane's order. Three frozen T2-05 files change in this commit, each with its `Frozen-Test-Change` trailer; the file list of `docs/specs/T2-05.tests.json` is unchanged, so the manifest is not edited. No backlog entry is taken or appended, no task is added, moved or dropped, and no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited. The owner can veto any of it.

- [workflow.md:2b (frozen tests); acceptance 1] Yes: the three files are changed here, before the Tests step, assertion for assertion as the spec's "Frozen-test ruling needed" section lists, so the task does not block. (1) `playbackFailureForPlayerError` is a function taking a `PlayerErrorCode` (its table may be a `Record` inside `domain/playback-failure.ts`); the frozen files call it and import it from `../domain/playback-failure`, so the test writer and the implementer use that form and path. (2) `failureAction(error, log)` takes the sink as its second argument and `stopFailureAction()` takes none, as the spec has it. (3) The three files are red from this commit until milestone 2: they import a module and use fields that do not exist yet. That is the tests-first state, not a fault; the test writer does not list them in `docs/specs/T8-25.tests.json` (they stay T2-05's) and the implementer does not edit them. (4) This ruling answered a question, so the run's one frozen-test ruling (`WORKFLOW.md` 2b) is still there for a frozen T8-25 file that turns out wrong. (5) The flow lint only follows a manifest added on the branch, so it will not list these changes by itself: the Ship step names the three paths in the PR's Frozen tests section from this commit's trailers. | reason: each of the changed assertions contradicts acceptance item 1 ("carries a code from a closed list and no text"): they require a `string` in the state's `error` or in the action's `message`, and they stop compiling once the fields go, so no implementation can meet item 1 with them in place. Ruling now and not at Implement keeps the order failing-tests-first, saves a round trip the spec already foresees, and leaves the implementer a branch it can turn green. Nothing is weakened: every old assertion has a code-only counterpart, the leak and `[path]` checks move to the sink's one argument, and three checks are added (a later player error does not keep `stop-failed`; the state after an error event holds nothing but the code; no leak string is in the action). One departure from the spec's list, flagged: in `coordinator-errors.test.ts` the later failure is `server-error`, not `server-unreachable`, because the engine's `network` code already maps to `server-unreachable` and the same code twice would show nothing. | probe: `scripts/task.mjs show T8-25` item 1 reads "A failure in the state a screen reads carries a code from a closed list and no text from the player, the system, the server or an exception"; before the edit `session-state.test.ts` read `expect(state).toMatchObject({ error: "The player did not stop.", errorStage: "stop" });`, `coordinator-errors.test.ts` read `expect(afterFailure.error).toBe("Jellyfin request failed.");` and `error-strip.test.ts` read `expect(action.message).toContain("[path]");`; `docs/specs/T2-05.tests.json` lists all three under `files`; `git log -- docs/specs/T2-05.tests.json` gives `dc77d0c8 test(T2-05): freeze failing acceptance tests`, which is on `origin/main`, and `scripts/check-flow.mjs:187` reads `rev-list --reverse --no-merges ${mergeBase}..${tipSha}`; `.claude/workflows/task.js:635` reads `if (frozen.length) frozenRulingUsed = true;`; after the edit `vitest run` of the three files prints "Tests  5 failed | 5 passed (10)", the failures being the missing `../domain/playback-failure` module and the old action shape, and `pnpm lint` prints nothing.
- [frozen-test] packages/core/src/player/session-state.test.ts: changed. The first case expects `failure: playbackFailureForPlayerError("decoder-failed")` in place of `error` and `errorCode`; "marks a failed stop" dispatches `{ type: "failure", code: "stop-failed" }`, expects `failure: "stop-failed"`, then the player error's code (and not `stop-failed`), then `server-unreachable` after a failure with that code. | reason: the case asserted text in the state and the `message` and `stage` fields that acceptance item 1 removes.
- [frozen-test] packages/core/src/player/coordinator-errors.test.ts: changed, the two cases of "the session state" only. The state holds `playbackFailureForPlayerError("decoder-unavailable")` and nothing else beside `firstFrameReady`; a later `{ type: "failure", code: "server-error" }` gives `server-error`; `reset` gives `undefined`. The "player error copy" case and every coordinator case are untouched. | reason: the cases asserted `state.error` text and `state.errorCode`, which acceptance item 1 removes; `PLAYER_ERROR_MESSAGES` stays as an `Error`'s message, which is not state (spec D6).
- [frozen-test] packages/core/src/jellyfin/error-strip.test.ts: changed, the `failureAction` block only. Every call passes a `vi.fn()` sink; a 500 gives `{ type: "failure", code: "server-error" }`; a native reason as a string or an `Error` gives `request-failed`, the sink is called once, and the `[path]` and leak checks read the sink's argument; a `PlayerStopError` gives `stop-failed`, and a string and an `Error` with the same text give one action; `stopFailureAction()` equals `{ type: "failure", code: "stop-failed" }`. | reason: the cases expected the filtered native reason and fixed text in the action's `message`, which acceptance items 1 and 4 send to the log path only.
## 2026-11-22, T8-01 ruling, Check

Two inline rulings on the Check step's questions for T8-01 (`docs/specs/T8-01.md`). No task is added, moved or dropped, and no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited. No frozen file is changed: `docs/specs/T8-01.tests.json` does not exist yet. One backlog line is appended (the T8 phase dependency re-check, for the next pass to place). Both rulings keep T8-01 first in the player lane, because it is on the path to first playback (T8-01, T8-02, T8-03). The owner can veto any of it.

- [acceptance 1] The spec's reading (D3) is accepted: T8-01 does not wait for T4-05. The session allowlist names all five members of decision 0005; `pending` names `restoreSessions` and `sessionEnded` with `T4-05`; the test requires that what the Kotlin defines equals the allowlist minus `pending`. Conditions: (a) `pending` may hold those two entries and no other, and each names the task that builds it; the player module's `pending` is empty; (b) a pending member that is defined fails the test, so T4-05 has to remove its two entries, and update `docs/security.md`, in the pull request that builds them; (c) the PR, the evidence and `docs/security.md` say three of the five exist and never claim five; (d) T8-01 builds neither member. | reason: item 1's "exactly" is about what the surface may hold, and its testable half is "a test fails when a method or event is added ... without updating that module's allowlist and docs/security.md"; the allowlist with a pending map holds that for all five names from today. The task list already orders it this way: T8-01 does not depend on T4-05, and T4-05 sits in another lane behind three tasks. Waiting would put a sign-in persistence task ahead of the slice to first playback for no gain in what the test proves. No acceptance edit is needed. | probe: `scripts/task.mjs show T8-01` gives `"depends_on": ["T2-05", "T3-03"]` and `show T4-05` gives `"status": "todo"`, `"lane": "native-session"`, `"depends_on": ["T4-03", "T2-01", "T3-21"]`; `grep "AsyncFunction(\|Events(" modules/session/android/src/main` finds three lines, `AsyncFunction("signIn")`, `AsyncFunction("request")` and `AsyncFunction("endSession")` in `OctaveSessionModule.kt:30-34`, and no `Events(`; `docs/decisions/0005-token-boundary.md:249` reads "The session module's allowlist is exactly `signIn`, `request`, `restoreSessions` and `endSession`, plus its one event, `sessionEnded`."; `docs/security.md:21` reads "Three of the five members exist (T3-05) ... `restoreSessions` and `sessionEnded` are T4-05's."
- [agents.md:dependencies] Deferring is accepted: the T8 phase re-check does not have to be done before T8-01 ships, because T8-01 adopts no dependency and changes no pin. T8-01's spec keeps the word "unverified" and its PR lists the re-check as not done. The re-check is due before T8-02's implement step, since T8-02 is the first T8 task that adopts a dependency (Media3) and builds on the OkHttp pin. Who: the refiner of T8-02, in the "Dependencies" section of `docs/specs/T8-02.md`, from primary sources, covering Media3 and the pins the player path builds on (the Expo module core, OkHttp, the JUnit BOM); if that step has no network access it returns the re-check as a question and T8-02 does not go to implement on unverified pins. Whether the re-check of every other pin becomes a task of its own or stays in T8-02's spec is a task-list matter: a backlog line is appended for the next pass. | reason: the rule exists to stop work building on a dead dependency; T8-01 builds only on workspace code and pins already checked in earlier specs, so holding it would be process ahead of the product. The phase has in fact already started without a record (two T8 tasks are done), so the gap is not T8-01's to close alone, and the honest place for it is the first adoption. This role cannot edit T8-02's acceptance inline, hence the backlog line. | probe: `scripts/task.mjs list` shows `T8-22 done` and `T8-24 done` with every other T8 task `todo`; `grep -rnil "re-check" docs tasks WORKFLOW.md` finds task specs, decisions and plans but no T8 phase record, and `grep -n -i "phase start\|re-check" WORKFLOW.md docs/commands.md` prints nothing; `docs/specs/T8-01.md` ("Dependencies") reads "No dependency is adopted, so no maintenance check is due." and "Media3 is **not** added; T8-02 adopts it and records its check."; `grep -n "T8-01" tasks/backlog.jsonl` finds lines 88 and 160 only, neither about the re-check, so the line the spec promised was not there.

## 2026-11-23, T8-01 ruling, Implement

Three inline rulings on the Implement step's report for T8-01 (`docs/specs/T8-01.md`). No task is added, moved or dropped, and no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited. Two frozen files are changed, each by one line, because neither compiled; the manifest's file list is unchanged. No backlog line is appended: nothing here needs a task-list or acceptance edit. All three keep T8-01 moving, because it is the first task on the path to first playback (T8-01, T8-02, T8-03). The owner can veto any of it.

- [frozen-test] modules/player/android/src/test/java/dev/octave/player/seam/PlayerEventMapsTest.kt: changed, line 201 only: a third `asMap(` is added, so the line reads `asMap(asMap(asMap(PlayerEventMaps.toMap(...))["snapshot"])["diagnostics"])["decodeLabel"]`. What the line asserts is unchanged (a `decodeLabel` of exactly 256 characters is written whole). | reason: the line had one closing parenthesis too many and is a Kotlin syntax error, so the file contradicted the spec's "The frozen JVM tests compile against these names" and stopped every player JVM test, item 4's among them; the path it reads (`snapshot`, `diagnostics`, `decodeLabel`) is the port's and the spec's, and no implementation could have made the line compile. Probe: after both changes `node scripts/tv-native-test-only.mjs 'dev.octave.player.*'` prints "tv native: 51 tests, 0 failed, 5 projects (filter dev.octave.player.*)".
- [frozen-test] modules/player/android/src/test/java/dev/octave/player/seam/FakeEngine.kt: changed, one line added under line 11: `private set` on `listener`. | reason: the public `var listener` generated a JVM `setListener(Function1)` that clashes with the `override fun setListener` at line 22, and the spec fixes that member's name and type in `PlaybackEngine` ("The Kotlin seam"), so the fake contradicted the spec and the interface could not give way. The tests only read the property (`PlayerSeamTest.kt:51`, `assertNull(engine.listener, "no listener before initialize")`, is the one use outside the fake), and the fake still assigns it inside its own `setListener`, so no assertion is weakened.
- [docs/specs/t8-01.md:risks and out of scope] (c) is taken; (a) and (b) are refused. T8-01 changes how `scripts/tv-apk-dex-mapping.mjs` reads an outline call site, in milestone 3, and adds no row, no keep rule and no list entry. The rule: a stack whose range is one line, where that line is a value of the `positions` of a `com.android.tools.r8.outlineCallsite` comment on a stack of the same dex method, is read with that call-site stack's outer frames as its outer frames, so D7 and D4 judge it by the method R8 took the outline from. Limits, all fail closed: only a line such a comment of the same dex method lists is read this way; a position whose call-site stack itself has a foreign outermost frame still fails D7; every other stack is read as before; `mixedClasses`, the lists and the keep rule in `apps/tv/app.config.ts` are not edited. With it come unfrozen tests cut from this build's mapping (this shape passes D7; the same lines without the comment fail D7; a position under a call site whose outermost frame is not `dev.octave.` fails D7), one sentence in `docs/security.md` ("What the dex check cannot see" or the D7 paragraph) saying how an outline position is attributed, an entry in the spec's implementation record, and a line in the PR's call-outs, since this changes a check CI runs. No frozen T3-19 file changes; if one of them fails under the new reading, stop and return it. Decision log 27 of `docs/specs/T3-19.md` ("A D7 line: the keep rule is restored, not the judge") stands for the case it names, a lost keep rule; here the keep rule is in place and the reader mis-attributes lines, which that entry did not foresee. | reason: the D7 line is not true of the dex: `OctavePlayerModule.definition()` is an Octave method with library code inlined into it, which D7 allows; the five lines it trips on are R8's bookkeeping for an outline taken from that method. A keep rule on Expo's `registerViewDefinition` (a or b) would change what R8 emits to suit the reader, name an Expo internal that an upgrade can rename, and leave the same failure waiting for the next library method inlined into Octave code and then outlined, most likely in T8-02 with Media3, the next task on the path to first playback. Fixing the reader fixes the cause once and makes D7 and D4 more exact, not looser. | probe: read `apps/tv/app.config.ts:27`, the keep rule is present (`-keep,allowobfuscation,allowshrinking class dev.octave.** { *; }`); read the release mapping of this build (R8 9.2.14, mapping version 2.2), section `dev.octave.player.OctavePlayerModule -> yq1`: the stack at `1171:1174` is `InternalModuleDefinitionBuilder.registerViewDefinition(...):0:0 -> b`, then `definition():419 -> b`, then `OctavePlayerModule.definition():0 -> b`, and carries `{"id":"com.android.tools.r8.outlineCallsite","positions":{"1":1208,"3":1210,"6":1213,"9":1217,"12":1222},...}`; the lines `1208:1208`, `1210:1210`, `1213:1213`, `1217:1217`, `1222:1222` each hold `registerViewDefinition(...):79:79 -> b` alone. In classes that pass, the position lines carry the Octave frame (`dev.octave.session.http.SessionTransport`: `112:112:kotlin.Pair kotlin.TuplesKt.to(...):43:43 -> c` then `112:112:java.util.Map endSession(java.util.Map):300 -> c`). A throwaway script outside the repository that fed the whole mapping to `mappingBuilder` and listed every stack in a `dev.octave.` class with a foreign, unsynthesized outermost frame printed `{ bad: 5, badOutline: 5, classes: [ 'dev.octave.player.OctavePlayerModule' ] }`: those five lines are the whole D7 failure and each is an outline position. Unverified: why R8 wrote these five lines without their caller frames; no R8 source or document was read for it.

The second ruling loosens a security check by one named reference, so it is flagged for the owner's veto with the pull request.

Every other assertion is untouched: the TypeScript side, the host `player-*` files and `modules/player/package.json` still may not name media3, exoplayer or exo, and the seam package still imports nothing from androidx. Condition: T8-02's own item 1 test (`scripts/tv-player-media3.test.ts`, per the spec) holds that the Gradle file adds exactly the two Media3 artifacts and the OkHttp BOM at the session module's pin, so what the file may name is still pinned; if that test is not in T8-02's freeze commit, this returns here. | reason: the two assertions described T8-01's state ("no player library yet", the Gradle file's own comment), not an acceptance item: T8-01 item 6 says "nothing in the TypeScript side names Media3" and says nothing of the Gradle file, while T8-02 items 1 and 8 require Media3 in that file, so the frozen lines contradict the acceptance of the task that owns the file next. A second Gradle project under a name without the word (b) would keep the letter of the assertion and defeat it; a separate Expo module (c) is a new native module surface, an architecture change, for no gain. | probe: read `modules/player/android/build.gradle` line 2: "No engine and no player library yet (T8-02 adopts one)."; `node scripts/task.mjs show T8-01`, item 6: "Kotlin sits behind an interface that a Swift implementation can mirror later; nothing in the TypeScript side names Media3"; after the edit `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/tv-player-module.test.ts scripts/security-doc-t8-01.test.ts` prints "Test Files  2 passed (2)  Tests  19 passed (19)". `scripts/check-flow.mjs` reads only manifests added on the branch, so the T8-01 manifest on `main` does not gate this file mechanically; the trailer is still given.
- [docs/security.md:rule d4 of the dex check] Option (a) of open question 2 is taken, narrowed as written here; (b) and (c) are refused. The implementer builds exactly this and nothing wider. (1) The row: `APK_NETWORK_ENTRY_POINTS` gains one row, class `androidx.media3.datasource.okhttp.OkHttpDataSource`, sinks `S1`, `linkedPath: null`, with its reason (the spec's "Dex rows"). D2 and D3 judge it as any row. No row for a default data source: if the dex check reports another Media3 class holding a sink, stop and return it. (2) The D4 exception: a named list in `scripts/tv-apk-dex-lists.mjs` with one entry and its reason; `scripts/tv-apk-dex-d4.mjs` reads it. A reference passes D4 only when all of these hold: the frame making it is a real (not synthetic-only) frame whose original class is under `dev.octave.player.media3.`; the class it names is exactly `androidx.media3.datasource.okhttp.OkHttpDataSource$Factory`, attributed to that one original (a merged set that holds anything else is judged as today); and the reference is one of three shapes: a `new-instance` of that class, an `invoke-direct` of its constructor `<init>(Lokhttp3/Call$Factory;)V`, or that same constructor inlined by R8 into the Octave method (the next-inner frame is that class's `<init>`). Everything else fails D4 as before: any other member of `OkHttpDataSource$Factory` (`setUserAgent`, `setDefaultRequestProperties`, `setCacheControl`, `setContentTypePredicate`, `setTransferListener`, `createDataSource`); any reference to `OkHttpDataSource` itself or another nested class; a `check-cast`, `instance-of` or `const-class` of either; an Octave field, parameter or return typed as either (the engine holds the value as `DataSource.Factory`); the same pair from any other Octave package (`dev.octave.player` outside `.media3`, the session module, the app); and the pair from a synthetic-only site. An entry no reference in the APK uses fails the check, as a stale row fails D3, so the exception cannot outlive the engine. (3) The factory argument: the dex check does not trace which factory is handed over, and the exception does not claim it does. Three things hold it and stay as they are: decision log 14's rule (D1 fails Octave code that takes a `Call$Factory` from a class outside `dev.octave.`), which applies in `dev.octave.player.media3` unchanged; T8-02's item 1 source test (exactly one `OkHttpDataSource.Factory(`, whose argument is `OctaveHttp.callFactory(`, and no setter on it); and item 7's wire test (the header on every request, range requests included). (4) Before R8: `NetworkPathRules.kt` K7 gains `androidx/media3/datasource/okhttp/OkHttpDataSource` (`NetworkPathRulesCoverageTest` requires every row class there), with the same single exception: a class under `dev/octave/player/media3/` may hold that one constructor reference and the class name it needs, judged by member reference; a class anywhere else, and any other member or descriptor naming the type, fails K7. If the test's class reader cannot tell the constructor from another member, stop and return it; do not exempt by class name. If a source rule of `pnpm lint:network` refuses the call, stop and return it. (5) Tests, in new unfrozen files, each with a failing and a passing sample: the pair from a `dev.octave.player.media3.` class passes (plain and inlined); the same pair from `dev.octave.tv.` and from `dev.octave.player.` outside `.media3` fails; a setter, `createDataSource`, the outer class's constructor, a field typed as the factory, and a synthetic-only site each fail from inside the package; an unused entry fails; an Octave class in the package taking a `Call$Factory` from a foreign class still fails D1. No frozen T3-19, T3-29 or T3-31 file changes; if one goes red, stop and return it. (6) `docs/security.md`, same pull request, must say: Media3's OkHttp data source is on the entry-point list, and Octave's engine package may construct its factory, and do nothing else with it, over `OctaveHttp.callFactory(session)`; the exception by name (which package, which class, which constructor) and that D4 and K7 are otherwise unchanged; that the dex check does not prove which factory is passed, and what does (the three things in (3)); that media requests therefore take the one client's origin, redirect, proxy and cookie policy; and the one-client row's "media: Planned (T8-02)" becomes what T8-02's tests show, no more. A sentence a frozen doc test pins is not reworded; if one must be, stop and return it. (7) The PR's call-outs name this as a loosened security check and say exactly what got looser: one constructor of one library class, from one Octave package. (8) Back to the Product Owner, not widened by the implementer: a reference shape on the real APK that is none of the three (a cast Kotlin emits, a constructor R8 moves to a synthesized class), a second class or member the engine turns out to need, or a frozen test that fails. | reason: the hard rules ask that every server request go through the one authenticated client and that no player path exist that cannot send the header; `OkHttpDataSource` over `OctaveHttp.callFactory(session)` is that path, it is what item 1 ("over the one OkHttp client") and item 8 ("Media3's OkHttp data source is proven") describe, and the 2026-11-22 ruling on sink rule S1 said the player task would bring D4 back for exactly this class. Octave's own `DataSource` (b) would make item 8 describe something that does not ship, so it needs an acceptance edit, and it would have Octave write and maintain range, length and error handling that Media3 already tests, a larger surface to get wrong on the path every media byte takes. Listing the class with no sinks (c) hides a real sink from D2 and makes the list say something false. Refusing every setter keeps the exception from becoming a header bridge, which a hard rule forbids. | probe: read `scripts/tv-apk-dex-d4.mjs`: `judgeReference` reports `D4 ... references ${name} ... which ... is on APK_NETWORK_ENTRY_POINTS` for every listed hit, and the file holds no exception list; `scripts/tv-apk-dex-operand.mjs:14`: `export const matchesClass = (holder, name) => holder === name || holder.startsWith(`${name}$`);`, so the row for `OkHttpDataSource` also lists `OkHttpDataSource$Factory`; `NetworkPathRulesCoverageTest.kt` builds its probe as `ClassRefs("dev/octave/tv/Probe", ...)` and asserts K7 refuses each row class from it, so a K7 exception limited to `dev/octave/player/media3/` leaves that test green; `NetworkPathRules.kt` K7 is matched by `names.any { it.contains(prefix) }`, by name only, so (4) needs a member-level read. Unverified: Media3 1.11.1's bytecode (the constructor's descriptor is taken from the spec's source read, `docs/specs/T8-02.md` "Facts"), and how R8 emits the call in the release APK; (8) covers both.

## 2026-11-23, T8-07 ruling, Check

T8-08's wire test stays on the forced-limit sample: it proves the validating parser, not the source decision. | reason: item 6 is read with item 3, which the same task states as "a conversion answer becomes an HLS plan (played once T8-08 lands)"; what T8-07 owns is the probe and the requests it causes, and item 6's testable half is "asserts the requests sent", which a negotiation gives in full. ## 2026-11-23, T8-23 ruling, Check

Focus: three inline rulings inside a product task (T8-23, no stream the device cannot decode reaches the engine); none adds hardening or process work and no lane's order moves. This spends T8-23's one frozen-test ruling. Every edited test passes on the code as it is today and, by the reading below, under the spec's rules; what the edits stop asserting is named as a condition on T8-23's own tests. Two backlog lines are added. No task is added, moved or dropped, and no acceptance item, `human` or `verify` is edited.

- [docs/specs/t8-23.md:decision log (d5)] The narrower rule is taken, and D5 is rewritten to it. A copy of a stream counts as possible only when both hold: its `AllowVideoStreamCopy` or `AllowAudioStreamCopy` is not `false`, and the URL's `VideoCodec` or `AudioCodec` list (read with the `codecList` that `copiesEverything` uses) holds the source stream's codec; a source stream that states no codec counts as held (cannot tell is never can play). One more form, found by the probe: a `VideoCodec` whose value is exactly `copy`, in any case, is the pinned Jellyscale's way of saying the video is copied; then the copy is possible and is judged as the source's first video stream, and there is no video encode target to judge. Without this the spec's target rule reads `copy` as a codec no device decodes and refuses every conversion in which Jellyscale copies the video. A copy that is not possible leaves only the encode target to judge. Unchanged: a missing or empty list is cannot tell and not playable; an unprobed source (D4); a failed probe (a copy that is possible by this rule counts as not playable); the forced request; `decideForcedConversion`. Conditions: (1) each half has a core test: a source codec outside the list sends no forced request when the target decodes, and `VideoCodec=copy` with a source the device cannot play sends the forced request; (2) milestone 1's recorder adds one row per Jellyfin server for a source whose video codec is outside the list (the no-decoder sample under a streaming limit), in a file under the `encode-target-` prefix, with the master playlist's `CODECS`; if a recording shows a server copying a codec outside its list, the test writer stops and returns it; (3) where no recording can be made (Jellyscale copying the video with Octave's profile; a DTS or TrueHD track, which no generated sample holds), the test rewrites one value of a recorded answer, as the frozen tests already do, and the spec marks that row unverified by a recording; (4) the spec's Jellyscale rows state the two forms (`h264` is an encode, `copy` is a copy) as read from source. Not ruled and not added: narrowing further by the URL's bitrate, size or frame-rate limits. | reason: Octave plays the original whenever the device can, and a server that keeps the video and re-encodes only the audio is the better stream; D5 as written throws that away for every disc and every limited stream whose audio is outside the list (DTS, TrueHD) on a device without that decoder, and spends a request to do it. Both Jellyfin versions and the pinned Jellyscale refuse a copy of a codec outside the list, so the list is not an unverified promise on any of the three servers. D5 is also not the safe choice on Jellyscale it was taken for: Jellyscale ignores the forced flags (#224), so there the forced request ends in `stream copy not turned off`, a refusal of a stream that would have played. If a server ever copied outside its own list the engine's refusal is the guard, as it is under a failed probe. Entry 135 (the forced request always turns video copy off) stays on the backlog; this ruling does not touch rule 5's request. | probe: fetched `MediaBrowser.Controller/MediaEncoding/EncodingHelper.cs` at Jellyfin tags v10.11.7 and v12.4 and read both rules: v10.11.7 lines 2358-2359 and v12.4 lines 2467-2468, in `CanStreamCopyVideo`: "|| (state.SupportedVideoCodecs.Length != 0 && !state.SupportedVideoCodecs.Contains(videoStream.Codec, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)))"; v10.11.7 lines 2539-2540, in `CanStreamCopyAudio`, and v12.4 line 2680, in `GetAudioStreamCopyFailureReasons`: "|| !supportedAudioCodecs.Contains(audioStream.Codec, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase))". Read Jellyscale at the pinned commit `b920b193cd6f` (`git show` in a local checkout): `internal/playback/decide.go:406`: "if !ok || !slices.Contains(p.AudioCodecs, stream.Codec) || audioCodecString(stream) == \"\" ||"; `internal/playback/spec.go:113`: "p.Copy = strings.EqualFold(query.Get(\"videocodec\"), \"copy\")"; `spec.go`, `videoCodec`: "if p.Copy { return \"copy\" } return \"h264\"", and `Query` writes it as the URL's `VideoCodec`. Read `packages/core/src/jellyfin/source-acceptance.ts:236`: "if (!codecList(params, \"VideoCodec\").includes(video.Codec.toLowerCase())) return false;". Ran jq over `packages/core/tests/fixtures`: every recorded Jellyfin conversion URL carries `VideoCodec=h264,hevc,av1,vp9`, every recorded source codec is h264, hevc, av1, aac or ac3, and no file in the Jellyscale pin folder holds a `TranscodingUrl`, so neither half of the rule has a recorded answer today. Read `tasks/backlog.jsonl:135`: "Keep video stream copy when only the audio cannot play (the forced request turns both off today)." Two conditions, both inside the item and neither a new measurement: the Implementation record keeps the departures, the janky counts and the 50th percentile per run as it has them now, and the PR lists the 50th-percentile cost (21 to 23 ms against 16 to 19 ms) beside D4 for the owner's veto. The review's medium finding is answered by this ruling, not by a rerun. The frame harness is not committed in this task; what the run taught goes to the backlog for T3-12, whose script is the committed held-key measurement. | reason: the item asks that the preview does not make a held Down worse than T3-11 left it, and on every recorded figure but the median frame time the head is no worse: the janky count is lower in absolute terms (5 to 7 against 15 to 22), so the pass does not rest on the larger denominator alone, and the 90th, 95th and 99th percentiles are equal or lower. Option (b) is hardening that would not settle the open doubt: a rerun at 20 a second on a software-rendered emulator still mixes fade frames into the share, `sendevent` reaching this headless image is unverified, and a second measurement after a pass is what the Check ruling ruled out in the other direction. Option (c) would move the line after seeing the figures. ## 2026-11-24, T5-03 ruling, Review

Focus: one scope ruling inside a product task (T5-03, Home, on the slice to first playback); it adds no task, edits no acceptance and moves no lane's order. One backlog line is appended for the next pass (T5-04 takes line 349). Nothing goes to the owner now; the sentence for a whole-Home failure is the owner's and is asked when T5-04 is specified.

- [scope] T5-03 ships with Home's failed state as a plain surface. The copy and the focusable Retry are T5-04's, and T5-03 adds neither. The review's medium finding is answered by this ruling and by backlog line 349, not by a fix in this pull request. | backlog: line 349 ("Give Home a failure state with plain copy and a focusable retry when its load fails (T5-03 leaves a plain surface and no error).") | approval: none needed, no acceptance edit | reason: none of T5-03's ten items names an error state, and its spec puts it out of scope ("A failed list or detail is not shown in T5-03; T5-04 maps failures to the copy table"); T5-04 is the next task of the lane, depends on T5-03 and owns it ("A failed row shows one sentence and Retry in its place", "A lost connection shows a plain sentence naming the server", "Each state takes focus on its action and works with the D-pad alone"). Adding it here would need wording that does not exist: DESIGN.md's copy table has a "Home, empty" row and no Home error row, and no InlineError control is built, so T5-03 would have to invent copy, which is the owner's. WORKFLOW.md's "fix medium findings in the same pull request" covers a defect inside the task's acceptance; this finding is another task's acceptance, so the inline rule (keep the task inside its acceptance, send the extra to the backlog) decides. No hard rule is broken meanwhile: the failed state shows no text at all, so no transport message or status code reaches the screen, and Back works (next line).
- Can a user be stuck: no. The shell's Back listener does not depend on a focused view, so on a blank Home the first Back focuses the rail's current entry and the rail's other entries open the other screens; a second Back leaves the app. What is missing is a way to retry without leaving Home, and any sentence saying what happened; that is the gap T5-04 closes. Unverified on a device: this is read from the code, and Home is not on screen yet. | probe: `apps/tv/src/shell/back.ts`: "if (isRoot(route)) { if (!railOpen) return \"open-rail\"; return route.kind === \"home\" ? \"exit\" : \"go-home\"; }"; `apps/tv/src/shell/use-shell-back.ts`: one `BackHandler.addEventListener("hardwareBackPress", ...)` registered in the shell; `apps/tv/src/shell/app-shell.tsx`: "onOpenRail: () => focusView(entryViews.current.get(currentKey))"; `apps/tv/src/home/home-screen.tsx:74`: "if (services === undefined || state.phase !== \"ready\") return <View style={styles.screen} />;".
- Does the path to first playback show it: no. Nothing mounts Home yet (a search for `HomeScreen` under `apps/tv` finds only `src/home/`; backlog line 342 wires it into the app), and T8-03 item 7 ("From Home, one press on Resume reaches the first frame at the saved position") runs through the ready state. The `failed` phase is reached only when the load throws: one failed list does not reject (`packages/core/src/domain/home.ts`: "A list that fails is named in `failed` ... and the rest arrive (D7). An aborted signal rejects the whole load"). Note for T5-04's spec: when every list fails (a lost connection) Home is `ready` with no hero and both rows hidden, which is the same blank surface by another road; T5-04's items 3 and 4 cover it, and the whole-load `failed` phase belongs with item 4.
- Order: T5-04 should land before Home is used on a real TV for dogfood, since a server that is off then gives a blank Home. It does not need to land before T8-03; no reorder is made.
- Backlog: one line appended. `needsPass`: the next pass should mark line 349 as T5-04's rather than the phase's hardening task (its `why` says "for the phase's hardening task"), and, as a scope edit, may add to T5-04 that a Home load that fails as a whole shows item 4's state with Retry in place of the plain surface. The sentence itself needs the owner (a "Home, error" row in DESIGN.md's copy table).

S01E01.mkv" 330`; the device run ended "outcome=pause-timeout" with a start at 0 s, one progress at 9 s and a stop at 10 s.
- Blast radius, read before ruling: nothing depends on S01E01 being 10 s. `player-samples`, `player-original-file` and `player-capabilities` play films only; frozen `scripts/tv-wire-player.test.ts` pins the no-decoder sample's codec and no length; the tripwire reads ranges and reports fixed positions (0, 5, 6 and 10 s) for every sample; the recorders set positions as a share of the server's own `RunTimeTicks`; `setup-servers.sh` waits on item names and counts. Two effects are accepted: after a tripwire run S01E01 is left in progress at 10 s on Jellyscale where it was left watched (the recorders and `player-viewing` reset its state before they read it), and the committed recordings keep `RunTimeTicks` 100210000 for S01E01 until someone records again, when that one number changes; no test asserts an episode's recorded runtime (`home-hero-fixtures.test.ts` reads films only).
- The spec's Facts line and D10 steps 1 and 3 are corrected to 60 s and the 3 s to 54 s window (this commit); the test's timing is unchanged: pause at 12 s for 2 s, stop past 25 s. `tests/samples/README.md` states the exception. Left to the implementer: delete the old 10 s file, run `pnpm samples:generate`, rescan the fixture libraries, correct the "330 s" and "16.5 s of 330" comments in its own uncommitted `player-viewing.ts`, and run the device test. CI generates the samples fresh, so it gets the 60 s file by itself.
- After the edits: `vitest run scripts/tv-wire-player.test.ts scripts/tv-wire-viewing.test.ts` gives "Test Files  2 passed (2)  Tests  55 passed (55)"; the script run on the old sample stops with "verification failed: S01E01 is 10.021000 s, not 60 s; delete it and run this script again". The 60 s file itself was not generated or played here; item 8's evidence is the device run.

## 2026-11-26, T8-03 ruling, Review

Focus: two rulings on review findings inside the product task itself (T8-03, the slice to first playback); they add no task, edit no acceptance item, change no frozen file and no manifest, and move no lane's order. Three backlog lines are appended (lines 361 to 363). Lines 361 and 362 need a pass to add their tasks (`needsPass`). The reading of the owner's testing rules below stays flagged for the owner's veto.

- [agents.md:tests] The three frozen files stay as they are in T8-03; nothing in them is hardened and nothing is added to them. They are replaced by later tasks, not by this one: backlog line 361 moves the media-URL guard to lint and then retires `media-url-boundaries.test.tsx` and the cast scan of `player-engine-shape.test.tsx`; line 362 settles the wire judges' self-tests for the whole class and drops the listing case. | reason: a frozen test changes only when it contradicts the acceptance item or the spec, and these contradict neither (spec D14 and D9 ask for them), so no `Frozen-Test-Change` applies and the task's one frozen-test ruling is not needed. The reviewer is right that they are source scans and harness self-checks, which the owner's rules forbid and the 2026-11-25 ruling refused to extend; that ruling dropped a planned file before it was frozen, which is not open here. Trade-offs: removing them now would leave "Build media URLs only in Playback Negotiation" with no guard for `apps/tv` at all, and would reopen files frozen for a task whose items are green; hardening the cast scan for `as never` and `as any` would extend a forbidden kind of test; keeping them for good keeps the breach. A boundary rule belongs in lint, as the core's are (`pnpm lint:boundaries`), and lint is not a test. | probe: `media-url-boundaries.test.tsx:23-24` `FORBIDDEN_MODULES = ["jellyfin/hls-url", "player/launch-request", "jellyfin/device-profile"]` and `FORBIDDEN_TEXT` over file text, with 12 cases over in-memory samples that test `violationsOf` itself; `player-engine-shape.test.tsx:23` `CASTS` holds three patterns, each ending in `PlayerNative` or `OctavePlayerNative`, so `as never` and `as any` pass; `:7-9` `function joined(module: ReturnType<typeof loadPlayerModule>): Parameters<typeof createPlayerEngine>[0]` is held by `pnpm typecheck`; a search of `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `eslint.config.*` and `scripts/eslint-*.mjs` for `hls-url`, `device-profile`, `launch-request` and `buildStaticStreamUrl` finds nothing; `scripts/tv-wire-viewing.test.ts:374-385` reads `wire-tests.json`, calls `existsSync` on `player-viewing.ts` and looks for the text `"player-viewing"` in `run-wire.ts`; its other 25 cases call `judgeViewingCapture`, `judgeRun` and the recorder.
- [frozen-test] apps/tv/src/player/media-url-boundaries.test.tsx, apps/tv/src/host/player-engine-shape.test.tsx, scripts/tv-wire-viewing.test.ts: left as they are. | reason: the line above. The follow-ups step edits none of them, and its new unfrozen tests state behaviour only: no source scan, no check of a list or a file's existence, no test of a test tool.
- [acceptance 5] Item 5 covers a report that fails while the viewing goes on (a progress report, and a start report through the same queue, `playstate-reporter.ts:133`); it does not cover a failed Stopped report. No screen change is asked of T8-03, and the gap is backlog line 363. | reason: the item's own words need a viewing that goes on ("never stops playback"), and the sentence it names ends "Playback continues.", which would be false after Back or the end of a file. `DESIGN.md` §10 names the row "Progress report failed", and the OSD that carries the notice belongs to the player screen, which is gone or closing by then. The "Couldn't stop playback" screen is the owner's for a player that did not stop (backlog line 359), a different failure. Showing anything on Home would be new copy and a new place, outside this item. The cost is small: the server keeps the last progress report, at most 10 s behind, so item 4's 10 s still holds, and the session stays open on the server until it times out. | probe: `DESIGN.md:477` "| Progress report failed | n/a | Couldn't save your progress to {server}. Playback continues. | n/a |"; `DESIGN.md:471` "Playback error, the player did not stop (Back, Retry, or a track or limit change; owner decision 2026-11-19)"; `docs/specs/T8-03.md:73` "three failing progress reports: the notice text ... appears once"; `apps/tv/src/player/player-screen.tsx:30` "True from the first warning for OSD_NOTICE_MS, and never again while this screen is mounted: one screen is one viewing."; `viewing.ts:114` "Resolves once the Stopped report settled or hit its deadline; rejects when the engine did not stop."
- For the record, the follow-ups (a) to (e) are agreed. On (c): the code is right and the documents are corrected, since Home must refresh after a viewing whether or not the stop report arrived; the comment at `viewing.ts:134` ("after it ended in any way and its reports drained") is corrected with them. On (b): the fix must not hold the first press on Resume until the refresh lands, because one press to resume is the product's measure; start from the newer of the card's position and the refresh's.

## 2026-11-26 (Product Owner pass)

Focus: of the 8 tasks merged since the last pass, 6 moved the product forward (T3-11 artwork, T8-23 conversion judged against the device, T3-39 the blurred preview, T5-03 Home, T3-34 icons, T8-03 first playback) and 2 were hardening (T3-21 signing, T3-37 addresses out of script output); of 242 open entries, 42 are new and 4 older ones were reopened, and this pass took 6 into two tasks, placed 10 in tasks that exist, deferred 20 and dismissed 10; the other 196 stay as earlier passes decided them.

The first pass since T8-03 merged (PR #341): from Home, one press runs a viewing through the core and the engine, and its playstate reaches Jellyscale. The slice to first playback is built, so the rule that ordered every lane behind it has done its work. What ships next is stated below. The look and wording calls in this section are made under the delegation of `docs/specs/decisions-owner-chat-2026-11-25.md` (1) and written in `docs/specs/decisions-product-owner-2026-11-26.md`, which says plainly that they are the Product Owner's and not the owner's words. Two tasks are added, three are dropped, two are moved and ten are edited. No frozen file changes, and no `status`, `owner`, `passes`, `evidence`, `human`, `verify` or rule is touched. Entries are named by their line in `tasks/backlog.jsonl` at this commit. The owner can veto any of it.

### What ships next

The product is now a signed-in app a person can open. Until sign-in lands, a release build has no session and shows a placeholder, so nothing built so far can be used.

- **ui lane:** T4-02 (choose a server), T4-03 (Quick Connect, and the item that mounts the signed-in app so a release build reaches Home), T4-04 (password, the only way in to Jellyscale until it has Quick Connect), then T5-04 (Home's empty, loading and error states), T5-08 (Home after a viewing), T3-35 (the loader) and T5-05 (the card menu). The libraries (T6) and the title pages (T7) follow in their order.
- **player lane:** T8-04 (the player screen: transport, scrubber, Back), then T8-06 (the info sheet), T8-08 (converted streams), T8-09 (every conversion shown), T8-10 and T8-11 (tracks and subtitles), T8-12 (track changes on a conversion), then T8-05 (hold to scrub).
- **native-session lane:** T4-05 (stay signed in) as soon as T4-03 passes, then T4-01 (find servers) and T4-08. Native-session and player work is serial: when both lanes have a ready task, the coordinator starts T8-04 and T4-05 before T3-13 and T3-14, which are release conditions and not what a viewer is waiting for.
- **core lane:** nothing is ready. T10-07 is listed next but waits for Jellyscale #234 by its own first item; it is not started. T7-05, T8-13 and T8-18 open as their screens land.
- **After those:** the wire test of sign-in (T4-07), Settings (T9), the Shield checks, the dogfood week (T10-05) and the release (T11). The owner's own feedback is asked for when the dogfood week starts.

### The order, moved in two places

- Moved T3-35 (the Signal loader) to T5, after T5-08 | why: it was the next ready task of the ui lane, ahead of sign-in. It stays a launch task (the owner's answer of 2026-11-23), but a loader is secondary to a way in; `DESIGN.md` 7.17 keeps the ring until it is built.
- Moved T8-05 (hold to scrub) to after T8-12 | why: in the player lane it stood ahead of converted streams, tracks and subtitles. A viewer can live with 10 s steps for a while; a file that will not play, or plays with no subtitles, sends them to another client.

### T1-29, and the workflow tasks

- Re-scoped T1-29, `acceptance` | scope edit | the rule moves to WORKFLOW.md and the refiner, spec-checker and product-owner agent files; the `DESIGN.md` section 12 cleanup stays; the item that changed the workflow script's prompts and the item that added `pnpm test:workflow` cases are removed, and one item says the task touches neither the script nor its tests. A new item names who takes a new call: the Product Owner while the delegation lasts, the owner after. | why: the flow is run by hand and no workflow script runs a task, so prompts in that script reach nobody, and a test of a decide function nobody calls is a test of the harness, which the owner's testing rules forbid. It is not dismissed, because two things in it still serve the product: the sign-in tasks are next and read `DESIGN.md`, whose status line and Awaiting list still tell them to wait for calls the owner made weeks ago; and the rule is what stops a spec parking once the delegation ends. The brief for this pass suggested keeping the `pnpm test:workflow` cases; they are dropped for the reason above, and the coordinator or the owner can restore the item.
- Dropped T1-22 (the mechanical gates as commands), T1-23 (Ship's wait for CI) and T1-24 (pnpm check before Ship) | why: each changes a step of `.claude/workflows/task.js`, and the flow is now run by hand, so they would improve a script that runs nothing. The backlog lines they took stay marked as taken by them, which is to say dismissed with them. If the workflow script returns, the three tasks are in git at this commit's parent. This rests on the coordinator's statement that the script no longer runs; `WORKFLOW.md` still describes it, and a veto restores the tasks.
- Not changed: T1-25, T1-26, T1-21, T1-27 and T1-28. Each still does something by hand (PRODUCT.md, the backlog marks, the size of the task file, the looser-rule sentence, the direction review), but T1-27 and T1-28 carry `pnpm test:workflow` items of the same kind; the pass that sees one of them about to start re-scopes it as T1-29 was. T1-26 would have saved most of this pass's reading and is worth an idle workflow lane.

The decision's named model has React controls. | the cost, stated and not hidden: keys reach React at key up on the JavaScript thread and a held key is named after 300 ms; the time from a key to the controls is unmeasured on the Shield and the cheap box. The remedy is then one native key source feeding the same key rules, with its own decision record, so the spec keeps the key rules pure and one file reading the remote. The PR lists the measurement as an untested gate. - [wording; design.md §7.14] The two 10-second buttons' TalkBack labels are "Rewind 10 seconds" and "Forward 10 seconds". | reason: the implementer proposed "Back 10 seconds", but the top bar's control is "Back", and two controls that begin with the same word and do different things are hard to tell apart by ear. The words are heard, not seen, and name the action (`DESIGN.md` §10). No frozen case fixes either label (`player-chrome.test.tsx` reads the row's three labels and asserts only the middle one). | for the owner's veto: item 11 of the spec's list.

Backlog line 374 carries the hook's fix, after which the player's guard goes. | a case for that first render is added in a new, unfrozen file.
- [spec D4; react-native-tvos 0.88.1-0] Confirmed, and now a spec line and a test: the screen reads a hardware key event only at key up (`eventKeyAction` 1) and drops every other. | reason and probe: `ReactAndroidHWInputDeviceHelper.handleKeyEvent` sends the long name when `!longPressEventStarted || eventKeyAction == KeyEvent.ACTION_UP`, so a hold sends it once on a key down and once on key up; `isSelectOrDPadEvent` alone starts the detection, so `longPlayPause` is never sent and a held play-pause key arrives once as `playPause`. The spec's Facts said only the key up carried the long name; D4 corrects it. A key down restarts no timer; what a hold means stays T8-05's. The frozen `pressRemoteKey` sends key up only, so the key-down cases go in a new file, `apps/tv/src/player/remote-key-up.test.tsx`.

## 2026-11-26, T4-02 ruling, Spec

Focus: eight rulings on the spec of T4-02, the first screen of sign-in, which is the product's next step after first playback. They add no task, edit no acceptance item, change no `verify` and change no frozen test. Four backlog lines are appended (371 to 374); two of them ask a pass for something. The wording and look calls are made under the delegation (`docs/specs/decisions-owner-chat-2026-11-25.md`, 1) and are listed in the spec under "Calls for the owner's veto".
