Spec only; no acceptance edit before the task runs. | reason: `DESIGN.md` 7.20 ("Without any found server, the field shows") and item 9 ("so the address field shows") state the product, and item 2's second clause says what Enter address does where it exists. A hidden field behind a button, on a screen with nothing else to do, is one press for nothing. | needs a pass: reword item 2 (backlog line 373); it does not block the task.
- [wording, copy table] Five rows are approved for `DESIGN.md` section 10; the sentences are in the spec's ruling R2. Redirect, unsupported version and "found server answered differently" reuse the core's existing sentences (the version one without the server's string). The unencrypted-connection dialog reuses decision 0001's warning with "Jellyfin password" made "password", the decision's own action label "Connect without encryption" (not the refiner's "Connect anyway"), Cancel primary and focused, and the server's name and exact origin under the title. | reason: a viewer already meets these words in the code's failures, and the dialog's text is an accepted decision. | for the owner's veto: **one sentence is new wording**, the row for text that is not an address ("That isn't a server address. Type it like …", with an example home-network address and port and an example name; the spec holds it in full). **One sentence of decision 0001 is left out**: "will remember this choice", because the choice is kept for one launch in this task and the sentence would not be true; it returns with persistence (backlog line 372).
- [look and wording, calls C3 to C9] Approved as the spec lists them: no section title over remembered rows, the conflict line on a found row (new wording), no Connect button (the keyboard's action key submits), "Loading…" while a probe runs, a failed discovery shows nothing, the origin chip's look. | reason: each stays inside `DESIGN.md` 7.20, which lists no Connect button and no section title. | the one to judge on a real TV is the missing Connect button: backlog line 374 asks for it at dogfooding.
- [design.md:back] Back during a probe cancels the probe and stays. Back with the field revealed does what Back does on the rest of the step, and the field stays. Back on the first step while signed out leaves the app with no question. | reason: a running probe is the topmost layer and can wait up to ten seconds on the device's capability report; the field is not a layer, so the step keeps one rule; the first step is the signed-out root, and `DESIGN.md` section 6 leaves from Home the same way.
- [frozen-test] apps/tv/src/shell/back.test.tsx: left as it is (lines 43 to 49, go-home from the sign-in route at depth 1). | reason: it does not contradict item 11. The test states the shell's pure rule; item 11 is about a signed-out viewer, who is never inside the shell, because the signed-out flow is its own composition with its own Back listener. The shell's sign-in route is reached only by a viewer already signed in (Add account, T4-06), and Home is right for that viewer. | probe: `back.test.tsx:44` "const WITHOUT_RAIL: Route[] = [{ kind: "notFound" }, { kind: "play", id: "x" }, { kind: "signIn" }];" and `:47` "toBe("go-home")"; `use-shell-back.ts:33` is the only caller of `decideBack`. The condition this rests on, that no task mounts the shell signed out, is written into T4-03's backlog line (371).
- [acceptance 4, 7] One launch is accepted: the confirmations and the remembered servers live in memory, so a viewer who relaunches is asked again and finds no remembered servers. "Once" and "a later visit" hold within a launch. | reason: until T4-05 a relaunch signs the viewer out anyway, so this costs one press on a path already being walked, and with the "remember" sentence left out the screen promises nothing it does not keep. Persisting needs a new native method (an architecture change), which is not this task. | needs a pass: persistence is not T4-05's by default, because decision 0001 section 7 says the confirmation "must never be stored with or derived from a token", so it cannot be part of the Keystore record. Backlog line 372; the lean is T4-06, whose saved accounts are the remembered servers.
- [verify] `verify` stays `pnpm check`. Item 8's Kotlin half runs under `pnpm tv:native:test` in CI's `tv` job, and the verify step quotes that job and the single-class run. | reason: a `verify` edit needs an approval file and buys nothing when no Kotlin main source changes. The Kotlin class is expected to pass on its first run, since the native client already refuses every address that fails the plain-ASCII test, so it is agreement evidence written with the implementation and not a red entry of the frozen manifest; item 8's red entry is the core test (precedent: `docs/specs/T3-33.md:55`). One JVM test file with no main-source change is not native work for the serial slot. | probe: `ServerAddress.kt` "// 2. R1 for https as well" then "if (!HostClassifier.hasPlainAsciiAuthority(written)) refuse(HttpRefusal.INVALID_ADDRESS)".
- [scope] `App.tsx` stays unchanged until T4-03. | reason: a step that hands on to nothing is a dead end in a release build, and T4-03 item 9 already owns the mount and the first-focus oracle. Backlog line 371 (taken by T4-03) carries the mount and the rule that the shell is never mounted signed out.
- [direction, call C7] A typed address with no scheme is completed with `http://` when the result is a loopback or home-network address and with `https://` otherwise; the completed address is what is probed and shown. | reason: most viewers type an address and a port; a public host gains no plain-HTTP path, and a home-network one still meets the confirmation.
- For the spec check: item 8 is the part to read hardest. The spec says the core and the native client refuse the same https addresses "by construction", and that the punycode form is accepted natively is unverified (no native test accepts one today); if an accepted vector is refused natively the task stops for a ruling.

## 2026-11-26, T4-02 ruling, Check

Focus: three rulings on the questions T4-02's spec check returned, and the check's first blocking issue fixed in the spec. They add no task, edit no acceptance item, change no `verify` and change no frozen test. One backlog line is appended (375), deferred. The spec holds the rulings as R9 to R12, with the probe's output.

- [scope, acceptance 8] T4-02 extends the Hermes conformance run (`apps/tv/src/e2e/conformance-run.ts`, judged by `scripts/tv-e2e.mjs`, CI's `tv` job) with the https-host vectors: every vector through the core's own address normaliser on the emulator, a refused one refused with its kind and an accepted one admitted with its expected server URL. The claim is not narrowed and not returned. | reason: item 8 says the punycode form "is accepted" and that the core and the native client agree, and the viewer is on the TV, where the core parses with Expo's URL library and not Node's. The hard rule about shared vectors names HLS validators and does not bind this; the item does. Narrowing would need an acceptance edit and a pass for something one section of an existing run proves. | probe (second question on item 8's evidence): the installed `whatwg-url-minimum@0.2.0`, which `apps/tv/node_modules/expo/package.json:238` depends on, run under Node on the fixture's three punycode vectors and three invalid-punycode ones: each parsed, with the hostname unchanged apart from lower-casing, none threw and none came back as Unicode; its README line 99 says "Revalidation of pre-existing `xn--` labels after UTS #46 mapping is intentionally omitted". So the device run is expected to pass from its first run: it is agreement evidence the verify step quotes, not a red entry. That was the library under Node, not under Hermes; the device run is what settles it. If it fails, the implementer stops and returns it.
- [workflow, acceptance 8] The test writer writes the Kotlin agreement test at the Tests step. It and the shared fixture are in the manifest's `files`; its single-class command is item 8's `also`, with a note that it is expected green at the freeze. This replaces the part of the earlier ruling (Spec, `verify`) that had the native engineer write it unfrozen in a milestone; that milestone is removed. | reason: an unfrozen test over a fixture the implementer can also edit is weak evidence for a security claim, and the red gate runs only `run` entries, so an expected-green `also` does not trip it (precedent: `docs/specs/T3-33.tests.json`, item 3). Each accepted vector carries the server URL the core must produce, and the Kotlin test parses that as well as the text as written, so it judges what the core hands over. | if it is red for an accepted vector at the Tests step, the test writer stops before the freeze and returns it; nobody changes the native address gate or drops the vector. `verify` stays `pnpm check`; no serial slot.
- [security, acceptance 8] The core gets no punycode validity check. A transport refusal of an https address whose host has a punycode label shows the "not an address" sentence, in place of "Something went wrong. Try again." | what the viewer experiences: with a core check, the "not an address" sentence before any request. With the residual as the spec had it, the native client refuses before any request and the viewer reads "Something went wrong. Try again.", where trying again can never work. As ruled, the viewer reads the "not an address" sentence either way, no request leaves the device, and the rule is one line of domain code. | reason: a decode-and-re-encode check cannot deliver agreement, because the native HTTP library also maps and validates the decoded label against its own table, so matching it means carrying that table in the core. Nobody reaches the class by accident: a server's real punycode name is valid, and a mistyped one is usually valid punycode for a name that does not exist. Item 8's "refuse the same addresses" is read in the item's subject, a host that is not plain ASCII; both sides refuse every such address by the same rule. | the fixture gains an invalid-punycode list: the core test asserts the core admits each, and the Kotlin test asserts the native gate either refuses it as an invalid address or keeps the host exactly as written, so nothing the native library decides about this class stops the task. | for the owner's veto: the reuse of the "not an address" sentence for this case. | later: a core check (backlog line 375).
- [spec, acceptance 11] The row of item 11 held the literal gate token in its last sentence, so `scripts/check-red.mjs` read the whole item as a review gate and its Back tests could not be its `run` or be frozen. Reworded: the Back test is the item's `run`, and the earlier ruling on the frozen shell test is read at the review step. | probe: `parseSpecGates` on the spec returned `{"11":"review"}` in the check and returns `{}` after the edit.
- [the check's other issues] All eight change a frozen test, the manifest or the list of files, so the refiner's revision settles every one before the Tests step and none is left to the implementer: 2, 3 and 4 as ruled above; 5, the dialog control gains one additive optional detail line for the server's name and origin (how it is drawn inside the control is the implementer's); 6, the dialog's Back case closes through the modal's own close request; 7, a probe cancels at once even while the gateway is still being built; 8, the two no-id cases are stated apart.
- Later | "Refuse in the core, before any request, an https address whose punycode labels the native client would refuse." (line 375) | returns if a viewer or the dogfood week meets a server name the core admits and the native client refuses | why: fail closed already, with a true sentence; an exact check means carrying the native library's IDNA table.
- For the coordinator: the thing most likely to change T4-02's size is the Kotlin agreement test going red at the Tests step for a valid punycode host. That the native HTTP library returns such a host unchanged is read from its source and unverified; a refusal there means a ruling and, most likely, a change to the native address gate, which is native work in the serial slot.

## 2026-11-26, T4-02 ruling, Implement

Focus: two rulings on test collisions the implementer met in T4-02, the first screen of sign-in. They add no task, edit no acceptance item and change no `verify`. One frozen test changes (a T8-07 scan). One backlog line is appended (376), deferred.

- [test, acceptance 10] `packages/core/src/jellyfin/gateway.test.ts:104-110` is not frozen, and the implementer updates its six cases in the same commit as the gateway change, as the spec's D10 says: each version fails with the kind `unsupported-version`, and neither the message nor any own property of the failure holds the version string. | reason: item 10 changes the behaviour the six cases state, so keeping them would pin the sentence the item removes; this is the changed behaviour's test, not a weakened one. | probe: no `docs/specs/*.tests.json` lists the path in `files`. The three manifests that match the text `gateway.test.ts` are T5-02 (`home-latest-gateway.test.ts`), T9-09 (`account-preferences-gateway.test.ts`) and T8-03 (`playback-gateway.test.ts` in `files`; `gateway.test.ts:681` only in item 3's `why`).
- [frozen-test] `scripts/tv-device-capabilities.test.ts` (frozen by T8-07): changed. The case "outside e2e/, only host/gateway.ts calls createGatewayFactory or names deviceCapabilities" now also leaves out any `test-support/` folder, and its title says so. Nothing else in the file changes. | reason: the case contradicts its own acceptance item for this file. T8-07 item 9 is "the full-capability default is never used in the app", and a test double is not the app: the scan already leaves out `*.test.*` for that reason, and `test-support/` is the same kind of file under another name. T4-02's frozen double builds the real gateway over a spy transport, which is what its spec asks ("so the real gateway runs"), so no product code could satisfy both frozen files. | not chosen: having the double build the gateway another way is a workaround around a scan and would mean changing T4-02's own frozen file; removing the case would drop a guard that does not contradict item 9 for product code. | probe: before the edit, `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/tv-device-capabilities.test.ts -t "T8-07 item 9:"` failed that one case with `+ "apps/tv/src/sign-in/test-support/sign-in-doubles.tsx"` against `["apps/tv/src/host/gateway.ts"]`; after it, the whole file passes, 37 of 37. No file outside `*.test.*` and `test-support/` imports from a `test-support/` folder today (grep over `apps/tv/src`), and `eslint.config.js:232` already treats `apps/tv/src/**/test-support/**` as test code.
- [workflow] This is T4-02's one frozen-test ruling (`WORKFLOW.md` 2, "rules once per task"), and it is spent. The Spec-step ruling on `apps/tv/src/shell/back.test.tsx` does not count: it answered item 11's own referral before the freeze and changed nothing. T4-02 can ship with this change: the pull request's Frozen tests section names the T8-07 file and this reason. A further frozen-test collision stops the task and returns it; the implementer does not get a second ruling inline.
- Later | "Add a lint rule: app code outside tests may not import from a test-support/ folder." (line 376) | returns if a review finds product code importing a double, or with the next lint or boundaries task | why: the scan now trusts that `test-support/` never ships, which holds today by convention and by review only.

## 2026-11-26, T4-02 ruling, Captures

Focus: one ruling on a layout defect T4-02's captures showed on the first screen of sign-in, the product's next step. It adds no task, edits no acceptance item, changes no `verify` and changes no frozen test. `DESIGN.md` gains a rule (section 9) under the delegation (`docs/specs/decisions-owner-chat-2026-11-25.md`, 1), listed as call C10 for the owner's veto. Three backlog lines are appended (377 to 379); one asks a pass for something. The spec holds the ruling as R13 and an amended D14.

- [look, DESIGN.md 9] A row or a field never scales, on focus or on press. A focused row shows the whole ring and its fill goes from `surface2` to `surface3`; a focused field shows the ring. Art and compact controls (cards, tiles, buttons, icon buttons, switches) keep the lift. | the defect: a focused server row read "emembered Fixture Server" with no left or right side to its ring, because the shared focus frame scales it by `lift` (1.08), about 68 px a side on a 1,696 px row, inside a scroll view that leaves 16 px. | not chosen: narrowing the column to make room for the lift. It would indent every list about 75 px a side from its heading, and the text of a focused row would still slide 68 px sideways, which section 5 rules out ("never ... slide across the screen"). In a 640 px picker sheet it would cost 33 px a side of a sheet that must fit two-line labels. | reason: the lift was specified for art (the token table's use was "Focused art") and `surface3` is already the token for a focused control's fill, so this uses what the design holds. It is the pattern for every later list: picker, menu, chapter and Settings rows, the text field and the search field. The rail's entries are left as T3-09 built them. | for the owner's veto: C10 in `DESIGN.md` section 12. The thing to judge on the TV is whether ring plus brighter fill reads as focus from the sofa without the lift.
- [look, DESIGN.md 9] Room for focus: a control's box at rest stays inside the title-safe inset, a column shares its heading's left edge, and no container clips a ring, a lift or a shadow. On the server step the rows, the field and the buttons start at x = 96 and the rows end at x = 1824; in the captures they started at 112, 16 px right of the heading, which was the scroll view's padding and not a design choice. The ring draws 7 px into the safe margin, as a card's lift already does. | reason: `DESIGN.md` section 6 keeps focusable things and text inside the inset, and a ring is neither.
- [scope] T4-02 changes the server row and the step's own field; the shared `TextField` keeps its default. | probe: `apps/tv/src/design/test-support/cases.tsx:29` lists TextField in `FOCUSABLE_CASES`, and `focus-motion.test.tsx:49` (frozen by T3-06) expects a scale on each case's focus; T4-02's one frozen-test ruling is spent ("T4-02 ruling, Implement"). So `FocusFrame` gains one additive optional prop for the still form, the server row and the step's field pass it, and the frozen file is untouched. | needs a pass: make the still form `TextField`'s only form and change the T3-06 case (backlog line 377). Until then a full-width field on another screen clips its focus; T4-04's password fields are the next.
- [tests] What catches the next one, in three parts. In this task: a component test that a focused server row and the step's field have the ring and no transform, written with the change. In every UI task from now: the capture check in the amended D14 (ring whole on four sides, no text cut, column on its heading's edge), run at verify and at review; backlog line 379 puts it in the two skills. Later: an on-device check of the focused control's drawn rectangle against its clipping ancestors (backlog line 378). | reason: a host component test cannot see a clip, since nothing is laid out there; only the device draws it, and the capture is the cheapest thing that does.
- [evidence] The "before" capture from the release build at the branch head is approved in place of the e2e build at `0488afcf`. | probe: `git diff --stat 0488afcf HEAD -- apps/tv/src/App.tsx apps/tv/src/first-screen apps/tv/src/design apps/tv/src/shell` lists two files, `design/controls/dialog.tsx` and `design/controls/text-field.tsx`, neither drawn by the first screen. The release app is also the truer "before": it is what a viewer has. The pull request names the build.
- [evidence] The three "after" captures are retaken after the fix, since each shows the column that moves, and a fourth is added with focus on the Server URL field, the other full-width control on the step.
- Dismiss | the placeholder first screen's first tile loses the left side of its ring and lift at the rail's edge (the same cause) | not changed in T4-02, and no backlog line: `App.tsx` and `first-screen/` are outside the task, the placeholder leaves each route with that route's task, and Home's rows already reserve the room (`rowInsetStart`, `apps/tv/src/design/rows/row-geometry.ts`).
- Later | "Add an on-device check to the e2e run ... fail when a clipping ancestor or the 1920x1080 frame cuts it." (line 378) | returns at the second clipped-focus defect, or with T3-10's focus test work | why: one defect, caught by the capture; the check is a medium piece of test tooling.
- Later | "Add to the change-review skill and the verify skill ... open each capture" (line 379) | returns at the next pass | why: T4-02's own spec carries the check; the skills are rule files, which a ruling does not edit.

## 2026-11-26, T4-02 ruling, Captures 2

Focus: two rulings on focus-drawing faults the retaken capture of the Server URL field showed, on the first screen of sign-in, the product's next step. They add no task, edit no acceptance item, change no `verify` and change no frozen test. `DESIGN.md` 7.19 gains two lines under the delegation (`docs/specs/decisions-owner-chat-2026-11-25.md`, 1), listed as calls C11 and C12 for the owner's veto. No backlog line: nothing is deferred. The spec holds the rulings as R14 and R15 and a second amendment of D14.

- [look, DESIGN.md 7.19, C11] The focus ring's 3 px offset is measured from a control's outer edge, border included, and the shared focus frame does it for every control: it reads its content box's border width and draws the ring that much further out. | the defect: the focused field's ring sat 1 px from the field (ring at x = 92 to 1827 against a box at 96 to 1824), because the ring is placed from the inside of the box's border and the field's 2 px `lineStrong` border used up 2 of the 3 px. | not chosen: fixing the field alone leaves the fault waiting for the search field and every later input; accepting 1 px contradicts `focusOffset` 3 and gives fields a tighter ring than the rows above them on the same screen. | reason: this is what 7.5 and section 8 already say, so it is a reading of the rule and not a new look. A control with no border draws exactly as before, so no other control's focused look changes. | probe: `grep -rn borderWidth apps/tv/src` outside tests lists `controls/text-field.tsx:75` and the ring's own two lines in `focus-frame.tsx`, so `TextField` is the only bordered control today; the search field is not built. `MediaCard` draws the ring itself on a frame with no border.
- [frozen-test] None changes, so the spent ruling is not needed. | probe: `focus-motion.test.tsx:46-48` (T3-06) reads `ringColor(ring)` and `ringWidth(ring)`, which `test-support/harness.tsx:67-73` take from `borderColor` and `borderWidth`; no file in `docs/specs/T3-06.tests.json` reads the ring's `top`, `left`, `right` or `bottom`. The new test of the offset is written with the change and is not frozen.
- [look, DESIGN.md 7.19, C12] A field's cursor is `text1`; selected text is marked with `fillSecondary` and a selection handle is `text1`. | the defect: the cursor was teal, the platform's colour, which the field never set. | reason: section 1 says the chrome is colourless and colour comes only from the artwork, so the platform default is not acceptable. `text1` is the colour of the text the cursor sits in. `focus` was not chosen: section 3.1 keeps it for the ring ("used only for focus. Not for selection, progress, or links"). The selection colours are set with it because the same platform teal draws them, and `fillSecondary` is `text1` at .12, which keeps selected text readable. No new token. | probe: `react-native-tvos` 0.88.1 declares `cursorColor`, `selectionColor` and `selectionHandleColor` on the text input (`types_generated/Libraries/Components/TextInput/TextInput.flow.d.ts:234,241`; `ReactTextInputManager.kt:370,427`). That the cursor then draws in `text1` on the device is unverified until the retaken capture.
- [evidence] `after-none-found-field.png` is retaken after both fixes; the other three after-captures stand, since none draws a bordered control in focus or a cursor. The capture check gains one line: the gap between a focused control's outer edge and its ring is 3 px.
- [scope] Both fixes are in T4-02: the capture is this task's evidence and the capture check calls a failing capture a finding. Both are a few lines in two design files the task already changed, with no native change.
- What inherits: every `TextField` on every screen (T4-04's username and password fields next) gets both fixes with no change of its own; the search field gets the offset when it is built on the shared frame, and 7.19's cursor line binds it. Backlog line 377 (the still form as the field's only form) is unchanged.


## 2026-11-26, T4-03 ruling, Spec

Focus: six rulings, in one batch, on the spec of T4-03, Quick Connect sign-in and the mount that lets a release build reach Home, which is the product's next step. They add no task, edit no acceptance item and change no `verify`. Two frozen files change (the task's one frozen-test ruling, now spent). Eight backlog lines are appended (390 to 397); one asks a pass for something. The look and wording calls are made under the delegation (`docs/specs/decisions-owner-chat-2026-11-25.md`, 1) and are listed in the spec under "Calls for the owner's veto"; nothing goes to the owner now. The spec holds the rulings as R1 to R6.

- [frozen-test; T3-01 item 4, T4-03 item 9] `scripts/tv-first-focus.test.ts` (frozen by T3-01) and `scripts/fixtures/tv/fake-smoke-adb.ts` (frozen by T3-30): changed. The first-focus oracle names the opening screen's own controls: `sign-in-search-again` holds focus with no key, the one press is Down, and `sign-in-address` is the first focus after it. Every other rule stays (the leanback resolve, exactly one press, no priming press, the tag check), and two cases are added: another control holding focus before the press fails, and the placeholder's old transcript fails. Item 9's "still passes" is read as the property, not the tile names. | reason: the frozen files contradict T4-03 item 9, which mounts sign-in from `App.tsx`. T3-01 item 4 is "its first screen takes focus from the D-pad without a priming press"; the tiles were the first screen of that day, and the first screen is now the server step, which has no tile and where Right moves nowhere. No honest change to the app passes the old cases: a side-by-side layout would reshape T4-02's ruled column to fit a test, and logging tile names from controls Right does not join would make the log lie. | not chosen: a judge that accepts any two different ids, which would pass a press that moved focus to the wrong place. | where the ruling differs from the refiner's: the judge keeps its one argument and holds the ids as a constant, so no other frozen caller changes; and the fake adb is a second frozen path the refiner did not name. | probe: `fake-smoke-adb.ts:56-60` printed `OCTAVE_FOCUS tile-0`, `OCTAVE_PRESS: 1 RIGHT`, `OCTAVE_FOCUS tile-1`, and `tv-dev-server-watch-controls.test.ts:152-156` (T3-30) expects `runSmoke` over it to equal `[]`, so the fake has to pass the judge; `server-step.tsx:137` draws `[searchButton, field, statusLine]` when nothing is listed, so Down from Search again reaches the field, and `host-sign-in.ts` wires the discovery that finds none; `tv-adb-judges.test.ts:26` feeds `OCTAVE_FOCUS [device]` and expects no focus, which the ruled id pattern (lower-case letters, digits, hyphens) keeps. After the edit `pnpm exec vitest run scripts/tv-first-focus.test.ts` prints "9 failed | 8 passed (17)" against the unchanged script: red until milestone 3 changes `tv-first-focus.mjs`, as a frozen test of the mount should be. | Frozen-Test-Change trailers on this commit, one per path. A further frozen-test collision stops the task and returns it.
- [scope, acceptance 7] The password slot holds a placeholder until T4-04; no password step is pulled into T4-03. The placeholder is the heading "Who is signing in?", "Not available yet." and Change server. | reason: a bare password step is T4-04's first item and its wire test is T4-07's; T4-04 depends on this task and is next in its lane, so the gap is one task long. | where the ruling differs from the refiner's: the placeholder leaves out "Quick Connect is off on this server. Ask its owner to turn it on, or use a password.", which offers a password the placeholder does not have and says "off" of a server with no Quick Connect at all (the pinned Jellyscale answers `false` from a stub). Every word shown is already in `DESIGN.md`. | accepted cost: until T4-04 a release build cannot sign in to a server without Quick Connect, the pinned Jellyscale included.
- [dependency] `uqr` 0.1.3, pinned exactly, in `apps/tv` only. | probe, 2026-11-26: `npm view uqr` prints version 0.1.3, modified 2026-04-03, MIT, no dependencies; `gh api repos/unjs/uqr` prints `archived: false`, `pushed_at: 2026-04-03`, `open_issues_count: 0`; its commits before the release are "fix(svg): escape color attributes in renderSVG to prevent SVG/XML injection (#10)" (2026-04-03) and "docs: add alternatives section (#12)" (2026-03-16); `src/qrcode.ts:723` converts with `encodeURI` and the file holds no `TextEncoder`. | reason: not archived, no open issue, and its newest release carries a fix merged the same day, so it is maintained; what counts against it is the gap from August 2023 to April 2026 between releases, which a finished encoder of a fixed standard explains. `lean-qr` (2.7.4, pushed 2026-11-10) is busier, but `src/qr/options/modes.mjs:98` builds a `TextEncoder` at module scope and nothing in this repository shows Hermes on the TV has one; proving that is work this task does not need. The check is recorded in the spec's Dependencies and is repeated when the next phase starts.
- [technical, acceptance 2 and 6] Confirmed: the client builds the approval link `{server}/web/#/quickconnect?code={code}` for every server that is not loopback; "the server gives no approval link" is the loopback case until T10-06; no tokenless route is added to ask whether a server hosts its web client, because the route list is decision 0005's and a new one is an architecture change. | the riskiest assumption, that the link opens a working approval page on a phone: partly read, not proven. probe: jellyfin-web `src/components/ConnectionRequired.tsx:87-88` at `v10.11.7` reads `const url = encodeURIComponent(location.pathname + location.search);` then navigates to its sign-in page with `&url=${url}`, and line 87 is the same at `v12.4`; the page reads `searchParams.get('code')` on mount. So a phone that is not signed in carries the code to the sign-in page. That the sign-in page returns to that `url`, and that a single-sign-on plugin's sign-in does, is unverified. | if it fails: T4-03 changes nothing. The code and "or enter the code in Quick Connect" are on screen and are the fallback, and a server that does not host the web client costs the viewer one dead scan. It is not a real-TV gate (no path into the home network, no leak of local information) and not an acceptance item: it is the owner's dogfood check, backlog line 393, and a failure there changes one function. | added at verify: `GET {fixture}/web/index.html` on both pinned Jellyfin servers from the host, the two status codes quoted. Evidence that the path exists, not a test.
- [look and wording, calls C1 to C8] Approved as the spec lists them: the heading and the with-QR sentence from the copy table; the no-link sentence "On your phone, open {address} and enter this code in Quick Connect."; "This code has expired. Get a new code to try again." and "The server didn't accept this code. Get a new code to try again." with the button "Get a new code"; "Try again" for unreachable and other; opening focus on Change server; the code grouped three and three and read digit by digit; a failure removes the code. | changed, C5: the QR code is 480 canvas px, not 360, with its 4-module quiet zone, `onLight` modules on a `text1` field, error correction M. reason: at 360 a 33-module code has 8 px modules, about 5 mm on a 55-inch panel, at the edge of what a phone reads from three metres; at 480 they are 11 px, and the step holds nothing else. Reasoned from the arithmetic, not measured. | changed, C7: the placeholder, as above. | for the owner's veto, the three to judge on the TV: whether the QR code reads from the sofa; that the opening focus is Change server, so a select pressed by habit returns to step 1; and that on a loopback server the no-link sentence names an address a phone cannot open (a developer's case today; line 395).
- [the two conflicts the refiner logged] Both are backlog lines and neither is T4-03's. T10-06 item 5 ("no token, secret or password in any URL") against the poll, which takes the secret in its query by the server's API: T4-03 does not own the item, T10-06 is blocked on Jellyscale #104, and rewording an acceptance item is a pass's (line 390, needs a pass). One Quick Connect device id for every account on a server, so a second account's sign-in replaces the first's token: with one account and nothing stored there is no second token until T4-06, whose spec weighs it (line 391).
- Later, needs a pass | "Product Owner pass: reword T10-06 item 5 …" (line 390) | returns at the next pass | why: above.
- Later | "T4-06's spec weighs the Quick Connect DeviceId …" (line 391) | returns at T4-06's spec | why: above.
- Later | "Send POST /Sessions/Capabilities/Full after sign-in" (line 392) | returns with remote control (T12-06), or sooner if a server feature a viewer sees needs it | why: no task names it and nothing on screen depends on it.
- Later | "Owner's dogfood check on the Shield: a release build signs in by QR code …" (line 393) | returns at the dogfood week | why: above; the owner is asked only for what needs the owner's eyes.
- Later | "Share one player engine adapter …" (line 394) | returns with the next task that changes the host's gateway wiring | why: tidiness; two adapters do not conflict.
- Later | "When T10-06 brings a server that gives no approval link and is not loopback, re-read the no-link sentence …" (line 395) | returns at T10-06's spec | why: above.
- Later | "Remove the placeholder first screen …" (line 396) | returns when the last route gets its own screen | why: it still stands in for routes that are not built.
- Later | "T4-01's and T4-05's specs keep pnpm tv:smoke's opening screen fixed" (line 397) | returns at each of those specs | why: the ruled oracle rests on nothing found and nothing stored; discovery and a restored session each change the first screen, and `adb install -r` keeps the app's data.
- For the coordinator: the thing most likely to make T4-03 bigger than it looks is milestone 3. `App.tsx` changes what every release build opens on, and five frozen host tests run the smoke over the fake adb (the T3-30 and T3-37 files); the probe read one of them. If another one pins the old transcript in a way this ruling did not see, the task stops for a second frozen-test ruling it does not have.
